,
with
C^{\prime}(0)
a lower triangular matrix whose diagonal entries are zero.
The integrability condition of
\nabla^{\prime}
yields

C^{\prime}(z){\rm d}z/z={\rm d}B^{\prime}(z)+[\tilde{A}(z),B^{\prime}(z)]{\rm d}z/z
.
Applying the transform
I_{r}-\epsilon B^{\prime}(z)
to the connection
\nabla^{\prime}_{U}
,
the matrix of connection becomes

\displaystyle(I_{r}+\epsilon B^{\prime}(z)){\rm d}(I_{r}-\epsilon B^{\prime}(z))+(I_{r}+\epsilon B^{\prime}(z))\big((\tilde{A}(z)+\epsilon C^{\prime}(z)){\rm d}z/z+B^{\prime}(z){\rm d}\epsilon\big)(I_{r}-\epsilon B^{\prime}(z))

\displaystyle=\tilde{A}{\rm d}z/z+\epsilon\big(C^{\prime}(z){\rm d}z/z-{\rm d}B^{\prime}(z)-[\tilde{A}(z),B^{\prime}(z)]{\rm d}z/z\big)-B^{\prime}(z){\rm d}\epsilon+B^{\prime}(z){\rm d}\epsilon=\tilde{A}(z){\rm d}z/z.

So
I_{r}-\epsilon B^{\prime}(z)
transforms

\big({\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}_{U},\nabla^{\prime}_{U},l^{\prime}_{U}\big)

to

\big({\mathcal{E}}^{v}_{U},\nabla^{v}_{U},l^{v}_{U}\big)
.
The transform
I_{r}-\epsilon B^{\prime}(z)
also preserves the parabolic structures
on the both sides.
Since the transform is uniquely determined by the
{\rm d}\epsilon
-coefficient,
we can see the uniqueness of the transform.
∎

The following lemma is essentially given in

[
15
, Theorem 6.2]
.

Lemma 10.4

(unramified irregular singular local horizontal lift)
.

Let
U
be an affine open subset of
{\mathcal{C}}_{\tilde{M}^{\prime}}

such that

\tilde{E}|_{U}\cong{\mathcal{O}}_{U}^{\oplus r}
,

{\mathcal{D}}_{\tilde{M^{\prime}}}\cap U=m^{\mathrm{un}}_{i}\big(\tilde{x}^{\mathrm{un}}_{i}\big)_{\tilde{M}^{\prime}}\cap U

for some
i

and that
\big(\tilde{x}^{\mathrm{un}}_{i}\big)_{\tilde{M}^{\prime}}\cap U
is defined by the equation
z_{U}=0

for a section
z
of
{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}}

on a Zariski open subset of
{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}
.
Then there exists a local horizontal lift

\big({\mathcal{E}}^{v}_{U},\nabla^{v}_{U},\ell^{v}_{U}\big)

of

\big(\tilde{E}|_{U},\tilde{\nabla}|_{U},\tilde{\ell}|_{U}\big)

with respect to
v
,
which is unique up to an isomorphism.

Proof.

We put
\tilde{x}:=\big(\tilde{x}^{\mathrm{un}}_{i}\big)_{\tilde{M}^{\prime}}\cap U

and
m:=m^{\mathrm{un}}_{i}
.
Write

\mu^{(i)}_{k}(z)=\sum_{j=0}^{m-2}a_{k,j}(z)\frac{{\rm d}z}{z^{m}}+c_{k}\frac{{\rm d}z}{z}.

We can write

I_{v}^{*}(a_{k,j})=a_{k,j}+\epsilon b_{k,j}\in{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}[v]}={\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}\times\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits\mathbb{C}[\epsilon]}={\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}\oplus\epsilon{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}.

We express the above equality by

I_{v}^{*}\mu^{(i)}_{k}(z)=\mu^{(i)}_{k}(z)+\epsilon\mu^{(i)}_{k,v}(z),\hskip 30.0pt\mu_{k,v}(z)=\sum_{j=0}^{m-2}b_{k,j}z^{j}\frac{{\rm d}z}{z^{m}}.

Take a local frame
e_{0},\dots,e_{r-1}
of
\tilde{E}|_{U}

such that
\tilde{\ell}_{k}\cap U
is given by

\langle e_{k}|_{\tilde{x}},\dots,e_{r-1}|_{\tilde{x}}\rangle
.
After a suitable replacement of the frame
e_{0},\dots,e_{r-1}
,
we can write

\tilde{\nabla}|_{U}={\rm d}+A(z){\rm d}z/z^{m}

such that

A(z){\rm d}z/z^{m}\pmod{z^{2m-1}{\rm d}z/z^{m}}
is the diagonal matrix
with the diagonal entries

\mu^{(i)}_{0},\dots,\mu^{(i)}_{r-1}
.
We can take a matrix
\tilde{A}(z)
with entries in
{\mathcal{O}}_{U[v]}

which is a lift of
A(z)
such that

\partial\tilde{A}/\partial\epsilon=0

and that

\tilde{A}(z){\rm d}z/z^{m}\big|_{z^{2m-1}=0}

is a diagonal matrix with the diagonal entries

\mu^{(i)}_{0},\dots,\mu^{(i)}_{r-1}
.
Set

B(z):=\int\begin{pmatrix}\mu^{(i)}_{0,v}(z)&0&0\\
0&\ddots&0\\
0&0&\mu^{(i)}_{r-1,v}(z)\end{pmatrix},\qquad C(z){\rm d}z/z^{m}:={\rm d}B+[A,B]\frac{{\rm d}z}{z^{m}}.

Then
\nabla^{v}_{U}:={\rm d}+(\tilde{A}(z)+\epsilon C(z)){\rm d}z/z^{m}+B{\rm d}\epsilon

defines an integrable connection on

{\mathcal{E}}^{v}_{U}={\mathcal{O}}_{U[v]}^{\oplus r}
.
By construction, the connection
\nabla^{v}_{U}

preserves the parabolic structure
\ell^{v}_{U}
on

{\mathcal{E}}^{v}_{U}
induced by
e_{0},\dots,e_{r-1}
.
So we can see the existence of the local horizontal lift

\big({\mathcal{E}}^{v},\nabla^{v},\ell^{v}\big)
.

Assume that
\big({\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}_{U},\nabla^{\prime}_{U},\ell^{\prime}_{U}\big)

is another local horizontal lift of

\big(\tilde{E}|_{U},\tilde{\nabla}|_{U},\tilde{\ell}|_{U}\big)
.
Note that
{{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}_{U}\cong{\mathcal{O}}_{U[v]}^{\oplus r}}
.
So we may write

\nabla^{\prime}_{U}={\rm d}+(\tilde{A}(z)+\epsilon C^{\prime}(z)){\rm d}z/z^{m}+B^{\prime}(z){\rm d}\epsilon

with
C^{\prime}(z)\equiv C(z)\pmod{z^{m}}
.
The integrability condition

C^{\prime}(z){\rm d}z/z^{m}:={\rm d}B^{\prime}+[A,B^{\prime}]\frac{{\rm d}z}{z^{m}}

(10.2)

yields
\big[A,z^{m-1}B^{\prime}\big]\equiv 0\pmod{z^{m-1}}
.
Since
A(z)|_{z^{2m-1}=0}
is a diagonal matrix whose constant term
A(0)
has distinct eigenvalues,
we can see that
z^{m-1}B^{\prime}|_{z^{m-1}=0}
is also a diagonal matrix.
Looking at (
10.2
) again
and using
C(z)\equiv C^{\prime}(z)\pmod{z^{m}}
,
we can see that

B^{\prime}(z)|_{z^{2m-1}=0}
is also a diagonal matrix
with the diagonal entries
\mu^{(i)}_{0,v},\dots,\mu^{(i)}_{r-1,v}
.
So
B(z)-B^{\prime}(z)
is a matrix of regular functions on
U
,
whose constant term is diagonal.
We can see by the same calculation as in the proof of
Lemma
10.3

that the automorphism

I_{r}+\epsilon(B-B^{\prime})
transforms

\nabla^{\prime}_{U}
to
\nabla^{v}_{U}

and it also preserves the parabolic structures on the both sides.
We can see that such an automorphism is unique,
because it is determined by the
d\epsilon
-coefficient
of
\nabla^{\prime}_{U}
.
∎

Lemma 10.5

(existence of ramified irregular singular local horizontal lift)
.

Let
U
be an affine open subset of
{\mathcal{C}}_{\tilde{M}^{\prime}}

such that

\tilde{E}|_{U}\cong{\mathcal{O}}_{U}^{\oplus r}
,

{\mathcal{D}}_{\tilde{M^{\prime}}}\cap U=m^{\mathrm{ram}}_{i}\big(\tilde{x}^{\mathrm{ram}}_{i}\big)_{\tilde{M}^{\prime}}\cap U

for some
i
and that

\big(\tilde{x}^{\mathrm{ram}}_{i}\big)_{\tilde{M}^{\prime}}\cap U

is defined by the equation
z_{U}=0

for a section
z
of
{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}}

on a Zariski open subset of
{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}
.
Then there exists a local horizontal lift

\big({\mathcal{E}}^{v}_{U},\nabla^{v}_{U},{\mathcal{V}}^{v}_{U}\big)

of

\big(\tilde{E}|_{U},\tilde{\nabla}|_{U},{\mathcal{V}}|_{U}\big)

with respect to
v
.

Proof.

Write
\tilde{x}=\big(\tilde{x}^{\mathrm{ram}}_{i}\big)_{\tilde{M}^{\prime}}\cap U

and
m=m^{\mathrm{ram}}_{i}
.
We denote the pullback of
\nu
via the trivial first projection

{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime}[v]\longrightarrow{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}\hookrightarrow{\mathcal{T}}

by the same symbol
\nu
.
As in the proof of Lemma
10.4
,
we express

\displaystyle I_{v}^{*}\nu(w)=\nu(w)+\epsilon\nu_{v}(w),\qquad\!\!\nu(w)=\sum_{k=0}^{r-1}\sum_{j=0}^{m-1}\!a_{k,j}z^{j}w^{k}\frac{{\rm d}z}{z^{m}},\qquad\!\!\nu_{v}(w)=\sum_{k=0}^{r-1}\sum_{j=0}^{m-2}\!b_{k,j}z^{j}w^{k}\frac{{\rm d}z}{z^{m}},

where
a_{1,0}\in{\mathcal{O}}_{\tilde{M}^{\prime}}^{\times}
and

a_{k,m-1}=0
for
1\leq k\leq r-1
.

We choose a local frame

e_{0},\dots,e_{r-1}
of
\tilde{E}|_{U}

whose restriction to
(2m-1)\tilde{x}
corresponds to

1,w,\dots,w^{r-1}

via the isomorphism

\tilde{E}|_{(2m-1)\tilde{x}}\cong{\mathcal{O}}_{\tilde{M}^{\prime}}[w]/\big(w^{(2m-1)r}\big)

given by Proposition
4.1

in the case
q=2m-1
.
Let

N\colon\ \tilde{E}|_{U}\longrightarrow\tilde{E}|_{U}

be the homomorphism
defined by the representation matrix

\begin{pmatrix}0&0&\cdots&0&z\\
1&0&\cdots&0&0\\
\vdots&\ddots&\ddots&\vdots&\vdots\\
0&\cdots&1&0&0\\
0&\cdots&0&1&0\end{pmatrix}

(10.3)

with respect to the basis

e_{0},\dots,e_{r-1}
of
\tilde{E}|_{U}
.
As in the proof of Theorem
7.5
,
we can construct homomorphisms

\theta\colon\tilde{E}_{\alpha}|_{m\tilde{x}}^{\vee}\longrightarrow\tilde{E}_{\alpha}|_{m\tilde{x}}

and

\kappa\colon E_{\alpha}|_{m\tilde{x}}\longrightarrow E_{\alpha}|_{m\tilde{x}}^{\vee}

which satisfy

{}^{t}\theta=\theta
,
{}^{t}\kappa=\kappa
and

N|_{m\tilde{x}}=\theta\circ\kappa
.
We may assume that
(\tilde{\theta}_{k})
and
(\tilde{\kappa}_{k})
are induced by

\theta
and
\kappa
, respectively.

Write

\tilde{\nabla}|_{\bar{U}_{\alpha}}={\rm d}+A(z)\frac{{\rm d}z}{z^{m}}

with respect to the frame
e_{0},\dots,e_{r-1}

of
\tilde{E}|_{U}\cong{\mathcal{O}}_{U}^{\oplus r}
.
Since

\big(\tilde{E}|_{(2m-1)\tilde{x}},\tilde{\nabla}|_{(2m-1)\tilde{x}}\big)\cong\big({\mathcal{O}}_{\tilde{M}^{\prime}}[w]/\big(w^{(2m-1)r}\big),\nabla_{\nu}\big)

as in Proposition
4.1
,
we can write

A(z)=\sum_{k=0}^{r-1}\sum_{l=0}^{m-1}a_{k,l}z^{l}N^{k}+z^{m-1}R_{r}+z^{2m-1}A^{\prime}(z)

(10.4)

for some matrix
A^{\prime}(z)
of regular functions,
where we are putting

R_{r}:=\begin{pmatrix}0&0&\cdots&0\\
0&\frac{1}{r}&\cdots&0\\
\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
0&0&\cdots&\frac{r-1}{r}\end{pmatrix}.

(10.5)

Set
{\mathcal{E}}^{v}_{U}:={\mathcal{O}}_{U[v]}^{\oplus r}
with
the identification

{\mathcal{E}}^{v}_{U}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{U[v]}/(\epsilon)=\tilde{E}|_{U}
.
Define the
{\mathcal{O}}_{U[v]}
-homomorphism

\tilde{N}\colon\ {\mathcal{E}}^{v}_{U}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{E}}^{v}_{U}

by the same matrix (
10.3
) as
N
.
Then
\big({\mathcal{E}}^{v}_{U},\tilde{N}\big)
becomes a lift of
\big(\tilde{E}|_{U},N\big)
.
Define matrices
\tilde{A}(z)
,
B(z)
,
C(z)
by setting

\displaystyle\tilde{A}(z):=\sum_{k=0}^{r-1}\sum_{l=0}^{m-1}a_{k,l}z^{l}\tilde{N}^{k}+z^{m-1}\tilde{R}_{r}+z^{2m-1}\tilde{A}^{\prime}(z),

\displaystyle B(z):=\sum_{k=0}^{r-1}\sum_{l=0}^{m-2}\frac{rb_{k,l}}{(-mr+lr+r+k)z^{m-l-1}}\tilde{N}^{k},

\displaystyle C(z)\frac{{\rm d}z}{z^{m}}:={\rm d}B(z)+[A(z),B(z)]\frac{{\rm d}z}{z^{m}},

where
\tilde{R}_{r}
is the endomorphisms of

{\mathcal{E}}^{v}_{U}
whose representation matrix with respect to the basis

e_{0},\dots,e_{r-1}
is the same as that of
R_{r}
in (
10.5
)
and
\tilde{A}^{\prime}(z)

is a lift of
A^{\prime}(z)
such that

\frac{\partial\tilde{A}^{\prime}(z)}{\partial\epsilon}=0
.
Using the calculations

\tilde{N}^{k}=\begin{pmatrix}0&\cdots&0&z&\cdots&0\\
\vdots&\ddots&\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
0&\cdots&0&0&\cdots&z\\
1&\cdots&0&0&\cdots&0\\
\vdots&\ddots&\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
0&\cdots&1&0&\cdots&0\end{pmatrix},\qquad\big[\tilde{R}_{r},\tilde{N}^{k}\big]=\begin{pmatrix}0&\cdots&0&-\frac{r-k}{r}z&\cdots&0\\
\vdots&\ddots&\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
0&\cdots&0&0&\cdots&-\frac{r-k}{r}z\\
\frac{k}{r}&\cdots&0&0&\cdots&0\\
\vdots&\ddots&\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
0&\cdots&\frac{k}{r}&0&\cdots&0\end{pmatrix},

we can check the equality

{\rm d}\tilde{N}^{k}+\left[\tilde{R}_{r}\frac{{\rm d}z}{z},\tilde{N}^{k}\right]=\frac{k}{r}\tilde{N}^{k}\frac{{\rm d}z}{z}.

(10.6)

Then we can see

\displaystyle\left({\rm d}B(z)+[A(z),B(z)]\frac{{\rm d}z}{z^{m}}\right)\Big|_{(2m-1)\tilde{x}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=0}^{r-1}\sum_{l=0}^{m-2}\frac{rb_{k,l}}{-mr+lr+r+k}\left({\rm d}\left(\frac{1}{z^{m-l-1}}\tilde{N}^{k}\right)+\left[\tilde{R}_{r}\frac{{\rm d}z}{z},\frac{1}{z^{m-l-1}}\tilde{N}^{k}\right]\right)\Big|_{(2m-1)\tilde{x}}

\displaystyle\qquad{}=\sum_{k=0}^{r-1}\sum_{l=0}^{m_{i}-2}\frac{rb_{k,l}}{-mr+lr+r+k}\left(\frac{-m+l+1}{z^{m-l}}\tilde{N}^{k}{\rm d}z+\frac{1}{z^{m-l-1}}\frac{k}{r}\tilde{N}^{k}\frac{{\rm d}z}{z}\right)\Big|_{(2m-1)\tilde{x}}

\displaystyle\qquad{}=\sum_{k=0}^{r-1}\sum_{l=0}^{m-2}\frac{r(-m+l+1)+k}{-mr+lr+r+k}\frac{b_{k,l}}{z^{m-l}}\tilde{N}^{k}{\rm d}z\bigg|_{(2m-1)\tilde{x}}=\nu_{v}(\tilde{N})\big|_{(2m-1)\tilde{x}}.

So the matrix

\big(\tilde{A}(z)+\epsilon C(z)\big)\frac{{\rm d}z}{z^{m}}+B(z){\rm d}\epsilon

determines an integrable connection

\nabla^{\mathrm{flat}}_{U[v]}\colon{\mathcal{O}}_{U[v]}^{\oplus r}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{O}}_{U[v]}^{\oplus r}\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}_{U[v]}}\tilde{\Omega}_{v}

such that the induced relative connection

\overline{\nabla^{\mathrm{flat}}_{U[v]}}\colon{\mathcal{O}}_{U[v]}^{\oplus r}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{O}}_{U[v]}^{\oplus r}\otimes\Omega^{1}_{U[v]/{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}[v]}({\mathcal{D}}_{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}\cap U)

satisfies

\overline{\nabla^{\mathrm{flat}}_{U[v]}}\big|_{(2m-1)\tilde{x}}=I_{v}^{*}\nu\big(\tilde{N}\big)\big|_{(2m-1)\tilde{x}}.

We can give a filtration

{\mathcal{O}}_{U[v]}^{\oplus r}\big|_{{\mathcal{D}}_{\tilde{M}^{\prime}[v]}}=V^{v}_{U,0}\supset V^{v}_{U,1}\supset\cdots\supset V^{v}_{U,r-1}\supset V^{v}_{U,r}=zV^{v}_{U,0}

by setting

V^{v}_{U,k}:=\mathop{\rm Im}\nolimits\big(\tilde{N}^{k}\big|_{{\mathcal{D}}_{\tilde{M}^{\prime}[v]}}\big)

for
k=0,1,\dots,r
.
So we can see that

\{V^{v}_{U,k}\}
induces

\overline{V}^{v}_{U,k}
,
\overline{W}^{v}_{U,k}

and that the homomorphism

\overline{\tilde{N}|_{V^{v}_{U,k}}}\colon\overline{V}^{v}_{U,k}\longrightarrow\overline{V}^{v}_{U,k}

induced by the restriction
\tilde{N}|_{V^{v}_{U,k}}

has a factorization

\overline{\tilde{N}|_{V^{v}_{U,k}}}\colon\ \overline{V}^{v}_{U,k}\xrightarrow{\kappa^{v}_{U,k}}\overline{W}^{v}_{U,k}\xrightarrow[\sim]{\theta^{v}_{U,k}}\overline{V}^{v}_{U,k}.

Then
\big(V^{v}_{U,k},\theta^{v}_{U,k},\kappa^{v}_{U,k}\big)

induces a factorized ramified structure

\big(V^{v}_{U,k},\vartheta^{v}_{U,k},\varkappa^{v}_{U,k}\big)

on
\big({\mathcal{E}}^{v}_{U},\overline{\nabla^{\mathrm{flat}}_{U[v]}}\big)
,
where
\overline{\nabla^{\mathrm{flat}}_{U[v]}}

is the relative connection induced by

\nabla^{\mathrm{flat}}_{U[v]}
.
Thus

\big({\mathcal{E}}^{v}_{U},,\nabla^{\mathrm{flat}}_{U[v]},\big\{V^{v}_{U,k},\vartheta^{v}_{U,k},\varkappa^{v}_{U,k}\big\}\big)

becomes a local horizontal lift of

\big(\tilde{E},\tilde{\nabla},\{\tilde{V}_{k},\tilde{\vartheta}_{k},\tilde{\varkappa}_{k}\}\big)\big|_{U}
.
∎

Lemma 10.6

(uniqueness of ramified irregular singular local horizontal lift)
.

Under the same assumption as
Lemma
10.5
,
a local horizontal lift

\big({\mathcal{E}}^{v}_{U},\nabla^{v}_{U},{\mathcal{V}}^{v}_{U}\big)

of

\big(\tilde{E}|_{U},\tilde{\nabla}|_{U},{\mathcal{V}}|_{U}\big)

with respect to
v

is unique up to an isomorphism.

Proof.

Let
\big({\mathcal{E}}^{v}_{U},\nabla^{\mathrm{flat}}_{U[v]},\big\{V^{v}_{U,k},\vartheta^{v}_{U,k},\varkappa^{v}_{U,k}\big\}\big)

be the local horizontal lift constructed in
Lemma
10.5
.
Take another local horizontal lift

\big({\mathcal{O}}_{U_{\alpha}[v]}^{\oplus r},\nabla^{\prime},\big\{V^{\prime}_{k},\vartheta^{\prime}_{k},\varkappa^{\prime}_{k}\big\}\big)
of

\big(\tilde{E},\tilde{\nabla},\big\{\tilde{V}_{k},\tilde{\vartheta}_{k},\tilde{\varkappa}_{k}\big\}\big)\big|_{U}
.
The connection

\nabla^{\prime}\colon{\mathcal{O}}_{U[v]}^{\oplus r}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{O}}_{U[v]}^{\oplus r}\otimes\Omega^{1}_{U[v]/M^{\prime}}(m(\tilde{x})_{M^{\prime}})

can be given by

\nabla^{\prime}\begin{pmatrix}f_{1}\\
\vdots\\
f_{r}\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}{\rm d}f_{1}\\
\vdots\\
{\rm d}f_{r}\end{pmatrix}+\left(\big(\tilde{A}(z)+\epsilon C^{\prime}(z)\big)\frac{{\rm d}z}{z^{m}}+B^{\prime}(z){\rm d}\epsilon\right)\begin{pmatrix}f_{1}\\
\vdots\\
f_{r}\end{pmatrix}

with
B^{\prime}(z)
a rational function on
U
admitting a pole at

z=0
of order at most
m-1
.
Note that
\nabla^{\prime}
satisfies the integrability condition

C^{\prime}(z)\frac{{\rm d}z}{z^{m}}={\rm d}B^{\prime}(z)+[A(z),B^{\prime}(z)]\frac{{\rm d}z}{z^{m}}.

(10.7)

Now we apply Proposition
4.1

in the case
q=2m-1
to the relative connection

\overline{\nabla^{\prime}}

on
{\mathcal{O}}_{U[v]}^{\oplus r}

induced by
\nabla^{\prime}
.
Then, after applying an automorphism of

{\mathcal{O}}_{U[v]}^{\oplus r}

of the form
I_{r}+\epsilon h
,
we may assume that

C^{\prime}(z)\frac{{\rm d}z}{z^{m}}\Big|_{(2m-1)\tilde{x}}=\nu_{v}\big(\tilde{N}\big)\big|_{(2m-1)\tilde{x}},

(10.8)

V^{\prime}_{k}=\mathop{\rm Im}\nolimits\big(\tilde{N}^{k}|_{m\tilde{x}}\big)

and that
\theta^{\prime}_{k}\circ\kappa^{\prime}_{k}
is induced by
the restriction
\tilde{N}|_{V^{\prime}_{k}}

for
0\leq k\leq r-1
.

By the equality (
10.7
),
we have
\big[A(z),z^{m-1}B^{\prime}(z)\big]\equiv 0\pmod{z^{m-1}}
.
Note that
A(z)
satisfies the equality
(
10.4
)
with
a_{1,0}\in{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{T}}^{\times}
and

B^{\prime}(z)\in M_{r}({\mathcal{O}}_{U}({\mathcal{D}}^{\prime}_{\tilde{M}^{\prime}}\cap U))

by the condition (ii) of Definition
10.1
.
So we can find
c_{0}(z),\dots,c_{r-1}(z)\in{\mathcal{O}}_{U[v]}

satisfying

z^{m-1}B^{\prime}(z)\equiv\sum_{k=0}^{r-1}c_{k}(z)\tilde{N}^{k}\quad\big({\rm mod}\ z^{m-1}\mathop{\rm End}\nolimits\big({\mathcal{O}}_{U_{\alpha}[v]}^{\oplus r}\big)\big)

since the equality

\ker\big(\mathrm{ad}\big(\tilde{N}|_{z=0}\big)\big)={\mathcal{O}}_{U[v]}\big[\tilde{N}|_{z=0}\big]

holds.
Then we can write

B^{\prime}(z)=\sum_{k=0}^{r-1}\frac{c_{k}(z)}{z^{m-1}}\tilde{N}^{k}+B_{m}(z).

with
B_{m}(z)
a matrix of regular functions.
Furthermore,
we can see that

B_{m}(0)
is a lower triangular matrix, since

\nabla^{v}={\rm d}+\big(\tilde{A}(z)+\epsilon C(z)\big)\frac{{\rm d}z}{z^{m}}+B^{\prime}(z){\rm d}\epsilon

preserves the filtration
(V^{v}_{k})
.
Looking at the equality (
10.7
)
again, we can see that

\displaystyle C^{\prime}(z){\rm d}z-z^{m}\left({\rm d}B^{\prime}(z)+[R_{r},B^{\prime}(z)]\frac{{\rm d}z}{z}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad{}=\big[\tilde{A}(z)-z^{m-1}R_{r},B^{\prime}(z)\big]{\rm d}z

\displaystyle\qquad{}=\left[\sum_{k=0}^{r-1}\sum_{l=0}^{m-1}a_{k,l}z^{l}\tilde{N}^{k}+z^{2m-1}\tilde{A}^{\prime},\sum_{k=0}^{r-1}\frac{c_{k}(z)}{z^{m-1}}\tilde{N}^{k}+B_{m}(z)\right]{\rm d}z

\displaystyle\qquad{}\equiv\sum_{k=0}^{r-1}\left[z^{m}c_{k}(z)\tilde{A}^{\prime}(z)-\sum_{l=0}^{m-1}a_{k,l}z^{l}B_{m}(z),\tilde{N}^{k}\right]{\rm d}z\in\mathrm{ad}\big(\tilde{N}\big){\rm d}z\quad\big({\rm mod}\ z^{2m-1}{\rm d}z\big).

In particular, we have

\mathop{\rm Tr}\nolimits\left(\tilde{N}^{l}\left(C^{\prime}(z){\rm d}z-z^{m}\left({\rm d}B^{\prime}(z)+[R_{r},B^{\prime}(z)]\frac{{\rm d}z}{z}\right)\right)\right)\equiv 0\quad\big({\rm mod}\ z^{2m-1}{\rm d}z\big)

(10.9)

for
0\leq l\leq r-1
.
Since

\displaystyle z^{m}\left({\rm d}B^{\prime}(z)+[R_{r},B^{\prime}(z)]\frac{{\rm d}z}{z}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad{}\equiv\sum_{k=0}^{r-1}z^{m}\left({\rm d}\left(\frac{c_{k}(z)}{z^{m-1}}\right)+\frac{kc_{k}(z)}{rz^{m}}{\rm d}z\right)\tilde{N}^{k}+z^{m-1}[R_{r},B_{m}(z)]{\rm d}z\quad\big({\rm mod}\ z^{m}{\rm d}z\big)

and since

[R_{r},B_{m}]|_{z=0}
is lower triangular nilpotent matrix,
we can see that the condition
(
10.9
)
implies

\displaystyle z^{m}\left({\rm d}\left(\frac{c_{0}(z)}{z^{m-1}}\right)-\nu_{0,v}(z)\right)\equiv 0\pmod{z^{m}},

\displaystyle z^{m+1}\left({\rm d}\left(\frac{c_{r-l}(z)}{z^{m-1}}\right)+\frac{(r-l)c_{r-l}(z)}{rz^{m}}{\rm d}z-\nu_{r-l,v}(z)\right)\equiv 0\pmod{z^{m}}\quad(1\leq l\leq r-1).

In other words, we have

{\rm d}\left(\frac{c_{0}(z)}{z^{m-1}}\right)\Big|_{m\tilde{x}}=\nu_{0,v}(z)\big|_{m\tilde{x}},\qquad\left({\rm d}\left(\frac{c_{k}(z)}{z^{m-1}}\right)+\frac{kc_{k}(z)}{rz^{m}}{\rm d}z\right)\Big|_{(m-1)\tilde{x}}=\nu_{k,v}(z)\big|_{(m-1)\tilde{x}}

for
1\leq k\leq r-1
,
which implies that

c_{k}(z)\equiv\sum_{l=0}^{m-2}\frac{rb_{k,l}}{-mr+lr+r+k}z^{l}\quad\big({\rm mod}\ z^{m-1}\big).

Thus

Q(z):=B(z)-B^{\prime}(z)

becomes
a matrix of regular functions.
Furthermore, (
10.7
)
and (
10.8
)
implies the equality

{\rm d}Q(z)+[A(z),Q(z)]\frac{{\rm d}z}{z^{m}}\equiv 0\quad\big({\rm mod}\ z^{2m-1}{\rm d}z/z^{m}\big),

from which we can see

Q(z)|_{m\tilde{x}}\in{\mathcal{O}}_{m\tilde{x}}\big[\tilde{N}\big]
.
If we apply the transform

I_{r}+\epsilon Q(z)
to the connection
\nabla^{\prime}
,
then the consequent connection has the matrix form

\displaystyle(I_{r}\!+\epsilon Q(z))^{-1}{\rm d}(I_{r}+\epsilon Q(z))+(I_{r}\!+\epsilon Q(z))^{-1}\!\left(\!\big(\tilde{A}(z)+\epsilon C^{\prime}(z)\big)\frac{{\rm d}z}{z^{m}}\!+B^{\prime}(z){\rm d}\epsilon\!\right)\!(I_{r}\!+\epsilon Q(z))

\displaystyle\qquad{}=(I_{r}-\epsilon Q(z))(\epsilon{\rm d}Q(z)+Q(z){\rm d}\epsilon)+\tilde{A}(z)\frac{{\rm d}z}{z^{m}}+\epsilon([A(z),Q(z)]+C^{\prime}(z))\frac{{\rm d}z}{z^{m}}+B^{\prime}(z){\rm d}\epsilon

\displaystyle\qquad{}=\tilde{A}(z)\frac{{\rm d}z}{z^{m}}+\epsilon\left({\rm d}B(z)-{\rm d}B^{\prime}(z)+\big([A(z),B(z)-B^{\prime}(z)]+C^{\prime}(z)\big)\frac{{\rm d}z}{z^{m}}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\quad{}+(Q(z)+B^{\prime}(z)){\rm d}\epsilon

\displaystyle\qquad{}=\tilde{A}(z)\frac{{\rm d}z}{z^{m}}+\epsilon\left({\rm d}B(z)+[A(z),B(z)]\frac{{\rm d}z}{z^{m}}-{\rm d}B^{\prime}(z)-[A(z),B^{\prime}(z)]\frac{{\rm d}z}{z^{m}}+C^{\prime}(z)\frac{{\rm d}z}{z^{m}}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\quad{}+B(z)){\rm d}\epsilon

\displaystyle\qquad{}=\big(\tilde{A}(z)+\epsilon C(z)\big)\frac{{\rm d}z}{z^{m}}+B(z){\rm d}\epsilon,

which means that

\big({\mathcal{O}}_{U_{\alpha}[v]}^{\oplus r},\nabla^{\prime}\big)

is isomorphic to

\big({\mathcal{O}}_{U_{\alpha}[v]}^{\oplus r},\nabla^{\mathrm{flat}}_{U_{\alpha}[v]}\big)

via
I_{r}+\epsilon Q(z)
.
Since
Q(z)|_{m\tilde{x}}
belongs to

{\mathcal{O}}_{m\tilde{x}}[\tilde{N}|_{m\tilde{x}}]
,
we can see that

I_{r}+\epsilon Q(z)
induces an isomorphism
which transforms

\big({\mathcal{O}}_{U[v]}^{\oplus r},\nabla^{\prime},\{V^{\prime}_{k},\vartheta^{\prime}_{k},\varkappa^{\prime}_{k}\}\big)

to

\big({\mathcal{O}}_{U[v]}^{\oplus r},\nabla^{\mathrm{flat}}_{U[v]},\big\{V^{v}_{k},\vartheta^{v}_{k},\varkappa^{v}_{k}\big\}\big)
.
We can see that such an isomorphism is unique,
because it is determined by
the coefficient of
d\epsilon
.
∎

Proposition 10.7
.

For any vector field

v\in H^{0}({\mathcal{T}^{\prime}},T_{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}})
,
there is a unique global horizontal lift

\big({\mathcal{E}}^{v},\nabla^{v},l^{v},\ell^{v},{\mathcal{V}}^{v}\big)

of

\big(\tilde{E},\tilde{\nabla},\tilde{l},\tilde{\ell},\tilde{\mathcal{V}}\big)_{\tilde{M}^{\prime}}
.

Proof.

We take an affine open covering

{\mathcal{C}}_{\tilde{M}^{\prime}}=\bigcup_{\alpha}U_{\alpha}

such that

\tilde{E}|_{U_{\alpha}}\cong{\mathcal{O}}_{U_{\alpha}}^{\oplus r}
for each
\alpha
.
We may assume that

\#\big\{\alpha\mid U_{\alpha}\supset\tilde{x}\big\}=1

for each

\tilde{x}=\big(\tilde{x}^{\mathrm{log}}_{i}\big)_{\tilde{M}^{\prime}}
,
\tilde{x}=\big(\tilde{x}^{\mathrm{un}}_{i}\big)_{\tilde{M}^{\prime}}

and
\tilde{x}=\big(\tilde{x}^{\mathrm{ram}}_{i}\big)_{\tilde{M}^{\prime}}
.
We may further assume that, for each
\alpha
,

U_{\alpha}\cap{\mathcal{D}}_{\tilde{M}^{\prime}}=\varnothing
holds or

U_{\alpha}\cap{\mathcal{D}}_{\tilde{M}^{\prime}}=\tilde{x}

holds for some
\tilde{x}=\big(\tilde{x}^{\mathrm{log}}_{i}\big)_{\tilde{M}^{\prime}}
,

\tilde{x}=\big(\tilde{x}^{\mathrm{un}}_{i}\big)_{\tilde{M}^{\prime}}

or
\tilde{x}=\big(\tilde{x}^{\mathrm{ram}}_{i}\big)_{\tilde{M}^{\prime}}
.

Let
U_{\alpha}[v]
be the open subscheme of
{\mathcal{C}}_{\tilde{M}^{\prime}[v]}

whose underlying set is
U_{\alpha}
.
If
U_{\alpha}\cap{\mathcal{D}}_{\tilde{M}^{\prime}}=\varnothing
,
then we can write

\tilde{\nabla}|_{U_{\alpha}}={\rm d}+A_{\alpha}(z){\rm d}z

for a matrix
A_{\alpha}
with values in
{\mathcal{O}}_{U_{\alpha}}
.
We can take a matrix
\tilde{A}_{\alpha}
with values in

{\mathcal{O}}_{U_{\alpha}}[v]
which is a lift of
A_{\alpha}
.
After adding an element of
\epsilon M_{r}({\mathcal{O}}_{U_{\alpha}})
,
we can assume that
\partial\tilde{A}_{\alpha}/\partial\epsilon=0
.
Then
\nabla_{\alpha}={\rm d}+\tilde{A}_{\alpha}{\rm d}z

is an integrable connection
and
\big({\mathcal{O}}_{U_{\alpha}[v]}^{\oplus r},\nabla_{\alpha}\big)

is a local horizontal lift of

\big(\tilde{E}|_{U_{\alpha}},\tilde{\nabla}|_{U_{\alpha}}\big)
.
Furthermore, we can prove the uniqueness of the local horizontal lift
by the same proof as Lemma
10.3
.

If
\alpha
satisfies

U_{\alpha}\cap{\mathcal{D}}_{\tilde{M}^{\prime}}=\tilde{x}

for some
\tilde{x}=\big(\tilde{x}^{\mathrm{log}}_{i}\big)_{\tilde{M}^{\prime}}
,

\tilde{x}=\big(\tilde{x}^{\mathrm{un}}_{i}\big)_{\tilde{M}^{\prime}}

or
\tilde{x}=\big(\tilde{x}^{\mathrm{ram}}_{i}\big)_{\tilde{M}^{\prime}}
,
we can take a local horizontal lift

\big({\mathcal{E}}^{v}_{U_{\alpha}},\nabla^{v}_{U_{\alpha}},l^{v}_{U_{\alpha}},\ell^{v}_{U_{\alpha}},{\mathcal{V}}^{v}_{U_{\alpha}}\big)

of

\big(\tilde{E}|_{U_{\alpha}},\tilde{\nabla}|_{U_{\alpha}},\tilde{l}|_{U_{\alpha}},\tilde{\ell}|_{U_{\alpha}},\tilde{\mathcal{V}}|_{U_{\alpha}}\big)

by Lemmas
10.3
,
10.4
and

10.5
.
Since the local horizontal lifts are unique up to unique isomorphisms,
we can patch them and get a global horizontal lift

\big({\mathcal{E}}^{v},\nabla^{v},l^{v},\ell^{v},{\mathcal{V}}^{v}\big)

of

\big(\tilde{E},\tilde{\nabla},\tilde{l},\tilde{\ell},\tilde{\mathcal{V}}\big)_{\tilde{M}^{\prime}}
,
which is unique up to an isomorphism.
∎

For a Zariski open subset

{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}\subset{\mathcal{T}}
,
consider a morphism

u\colon\ \mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}[\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}]/\big(\epsilon_{1}^{2},\epsilon_{2}^{2}\big)\longrightarrow{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}

such that

u|_{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}=\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}
.
Let

\bar{u}\colon\ \mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}[\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}]/\big(\epsilon_{1}^{2},\epsilon_{1}\epsilon_{2},\epsilon_{2}^{2}\big)\longrightarrow{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}

be the induced morphism which corresponds to a pair

(u_{1},u_{2})
of vector fields.
We write

{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}[\bar{u}]:={\mathcal{T}}^{\prime}\times\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits\mathbb{C}[\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}]/\big(\epsilon_{1}^{2},\epsilon_{1}\epsilon_{2},\epsilon_{2}^{2}\big),\qquad{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}[u]:={\mathcal{T}}^{\prime}\times\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits\mathbb{C}[\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}]/\big(\epsilon_{1}^{2},\epsilon_{2}^{2}\big)

with the structure morphisms

{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}[\bar{u}]\xrightarrow{\bar{u}}{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}

and

{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}[u]\xrightarrow{u}{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}
,
respectively.
We further set

\displaystyle\tilde{M^{\prime}}[\bar{u}]:=\tilde{M}^{\prime}\times_{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}[\bar{u}],\qquad

\displaystyle{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathcal{M}^{\prime}}[\bar{u}]}:={\mathcal{C}}\times_{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}{\mathcal{M}^{\prime}}[\bar{u}],

\displaystyle\tilde{M^{\prime}}[u]:=\tilde{M}^{\prime}\times_{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}[u],\qquad

\displaystyle{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathcal{M}^{\prime}}[u]}:={\mathcal{C}}\times_{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}{\mathcal{M}^{\prime}}[u].

We define a coherent subsheaf

\tilde{\Omega}_{u}
of

\Omega^{1}_{{\mathcal{C}}_{\tilde{M}^{\prime}[u]}/\tilde{M}^{\prime}}\big({\mathcal{D}}_{\tilde{M^{\prime}}[u]}\big)

in the same way as in (
10.1
)
and define a coherent subsheaf

\tilde{\Omega}_{\bar{u}}
of

\Omega^{1}_{{\mathcal{C}}_{\tilde{M}^{\prime}[\bar{u}]}/\tilde{M}^{\prime}}\big({\mathcal{D}}_{\tilde{M^{\prime}}[\bar{u}]}\big)

similarly.

Definition 10.8
.

We say that

\big({\mathcal{E}}^{u},\nabla^{u},l^{u},\ell^{u},{\mathcal{V}}^{u}\big)

(
resp.
\big({\mathcal{E}}^{\bar{u}},\nabla^{\bar{u}},l^{\bar{u}},\ell^{\bar{u}},{\mathcal{V}}^{\bar{u}}\big)
)

is a horizontal lift of

\big(\tilde{E},\tilde{\nabla},\tilde{l},\tilde{\ell},\tilde{\mathcal{V}}\big)_{\tilde{M}^{\prime}}

with respect to
u
(resp.
\bar{u}
)
if the conditions (i), (ii), (iii), (iv) and (v) of Definition
10.1

are satisfied after replacing

\tilde{M}^{\prime}[v]
with
\tilde{M^{\prime}}[u]
(resp.
\tilde{M^{\prime}}[\bar{u}]
),
replacing
\tilde{\Omega}_{v}
with
\tilde{\Omega}_{u}

(resp.
\tilde{\Omega}_{\bar{u}}
),
replacing
(\lambda,I_{v}^{*}\tilde{\mu},I_{v}^{*}\tilde{\nu})
-structure in (vi)
with
(\lambda,I_{u}^{*}\tilde{\mu},I_{u}^{*}\tilde{\nu})
-structure
(resp.
(\lambda,I_{\bar{u}}^{*}\tilde{\mu},I_{\bar{u}}^{*}\tilde{\nu})
-structure)
and replacing the equality of integrability condition in (iii) with

\displaystyle\frac{\partial A}{\partial\epsilon_{1}}{\rm d}z\wedge{\rm d}\epsilon_{1}+\frac{\partial A}{\partial\epsilon_{2}}{\rm d}z\wedge{\rm d}\epsilon_{2}+\frac{\partial B_{1}}{\partial\epsilon_{2}}{\rm d}\epsilon_{2}\wedge{\rm d}\epsilon_{1}+\frac{\partial B_{2}}{\partial\epsilon_{1}}{\rm d}\epsilon_{1}\wedge{\rm d}\epsilon_{2}

\displaystyle\qquad{}={\rm d}B_{1}\wedge{\rm d}\epsilon_{1}+[A,B_{1}]{\rm d}z\wedge{\rm d}\epsilon_{1}+{\rm d}B_{2}\wedge{\rm d}\epsilon_{1}+[A,B_{2}]{\rm d}z\wedge d\epsilon_{2}+[B_{1},B_{2}]{\rm d}\epsilon_{1}\wedge{\rm d}\epsilon_{2}

for

\Gamma^{u}=A{\rm d}z+B_{1}{\rm d}\epsilon_{1}+B_{2}{\rm d}\epsilon_{2}

(resp. replacing with

\displaystyle\frac{\partial A}{\partial\epsilon_{1}}{\rm d}z\wedge{\rm d}\epsilon_{1}+\frac{\partial A}{\partial\epsilon_{2}}{\rm d}z\wedge{\rm d}\epsilon_{2}={\rm d}B_{1}\wedge d\epsilon_{1}+[A,B_{1}]{\rm d}z\wedge{\rm d}\epsilon_{1}+{\rm d}B_{2}\wedge{\rm d}\epsilon_{1}+[A,B_{2}]{\rm d}z\wedge{\rm d}\epsilon_{2}

for

\Gamma^{\bar{u}}=Adz+B_{1}{\rm d}\epsilon_{1}+B_{2}{\rm d}\epsilon_{2}
).

The following proposition can be proved in the same way as
Proposition
10.7
.
So we omit its proof.

Proposition 10.9
.

There exists a unique horizontal lift

\big({\mathcal{E}}^{\bar{u}},\nabla^{\bar{u}},l^{\bar{u}},\ell^{\bar{u}},{\mathcal{V}}^{\bar{u}}\big)

of

\big(\tilde{E},\tilde{\nabla},\tilde{l},\tilde{\ell},\tilde{\mathcal{V}}\big)_{\tilde{M}^{\prime}}

with respect to

\bar{u}\colon{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}[\bar{u}]=\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}[\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}]/\big(\epsilon_{1}^{2},\epsilon_{1}\epsilon_{2},\epsilon_{2}^{2}\big)\longrightarrow{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}
.

If a horizontal lift

\big({\mathcal{E}}^{u},\nabla^{u},l^{u},\ell^{u},{\mathcal{V}}^{u}\big)

of

\big(\tilde{E},\tilde{\nabla},\tilde{l},\tilde{\ell},\tilde{\mathcal{V}}\big)_{\tilde{M}^{\prime}}

with respect to
u
exists,
it can be obtained as a lift of

\big({\mathcal{E}}^{\bar{u}},\nabla^{\bar{u}},l^{\bar{u}},\ell^{\bar{u}},{\mathcal{V}}^{\bar{u}}\big)

whose existence is ensured by Proposition
10.9
.

Proposition 10.10
.

There exists a unique horizontal lift

\big({\mathcal{E}}^{u},\nabla^{u},l^{u},\ell^{u},{\mathcal{V}}^{u}\big)

of

\big(\tilde{E},\tilde{\nabla},\tilde{l},\tilde{\ell},\tilde{\mathcal{V}}\big)_{\tilde{M}^{\prime}}

with respect to

u\colon{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}[u]=\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}[\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}]/\big(\epsilon_{1}^{2},\epsilon_{2}^{2}\big)\longrightarrow{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}
.

Proof.

By Proposition
10.9
,
there is a unique horizontal lift

\big({\mathcal{E}}^{\bar{u}}\!,\nabla^{\bar{u}}\!,l^{\bar{u}}\!,\ell^{\bar{u}}\!,{\mathcal{V}}^{\bar{u}}\big)

of

\big(\tilde{E},\tilde{\nabla},\tilde{l},\tilde{\ell},\tilde{\mathcal{V}}\big)_{\tilde{M}^{\prime}}

with respect to

\bar{u}\colon\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}[\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}]/\big(\epsilon_{1}^{2},\epsilon_{1}\epsilon_{2},\epsilon_{2}^{2}\big)\rightarrow{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}
.
So we only have to show the existence and the uniqueness of a lift of

\big({\mathcal{E}}^{\bar{u}},\nabla^{\bar{u}},l^{\bar{u}},\ell^{\bar{u}},{\mathcal{V}}^{\bar{u}}\big)
,
which is a horizontal lift of

\big(\tilde{E},\tilde{\nabla},\tilde{l},\tilde{\ell},\tilde{\mathcal{V}}\big)_{\tilde{M}^{\prime}}

with respect to the morphism

u\colon{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}[u]=\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}[\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}]/\big(\epsilon_{1}^{2},\epsilon_{2}^{2}\big)\longrightarrow{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}
.
The method of the proof is similar to that of
Proposition
10.7
.

We take an affine open covering

{\mathcal{C}}\times_{\mathcal{T}}\tilde{M}^{\prime}=\bigcup U_{\alpha}

as in the proof of Proposition
10.7
.
If
U_{\alpha}
is an open neighborhood of
\big(\tilde{x}_{i}^{\mathrm{un}}\big)_{\tilde{M}^{\prime}}
,
then the existence and the uniqueness of the local horizontal lift
with respect to
u
is given in the proof of
[
12
, Lemma 5.5]
.
If
U_{\alpha}
is an open neighborhood of

\big(\tilde{x}_{i}^{\mathrm{log}}\big)_{\tilde{M}^{\prime}}
,
then it is much easier to prove the existence and the uniqueness of
a logarithmic local horizontal lift.

So assume that
\tilde{x}:=\big(\tilde{x}_{i}^{\mathrm{ram}}\big)_{\tilde{M}^{\prime}}

is contained in
U_{\alpha}
.
If
u
is given by

\displaystyle u^{*}(\nu(w))

\displaystyle=\nu(w)+\epsilon_{1}\nu_{u_{1}}(w)+\epsilon_{2}\nu_{u_{2}}(w)+\epsilon_{1}\epsilon_{2}\nu_{u_{12}}(w)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=0}^{r-1}\left(a_{k,m-1}z^{m-1}+\sum_{l=0}^{m-2}(a_{k,l}+\epsilon_{1}b_{1,k,l}+\epsilon_{2}b_{2,k,l}+\epsilon_{1}\epsilon_{2}b_{1,2,k,l})z^{l}\right)w^{k},

then, by the proof of Proposition
10.7
,
the restriction of
\nabla^{\bar{u}}
to

U_{\alpha}[\bar{u}]=U_{\alpha}[u]\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}[u]/(\epsilon_{1}\epsilon_{2})}

can be given by

A(z)\frac{{\rm d}z}{z^{m}}+\epsilon_{1}C_{1}(z)\frac{{\rm d}z}{z^{m}}+\epsilon_{2}C_{2}(z)\frac{{\rm d}z}{z^{m}}+B_{1}(z){\rm d}\epsilon_{1}+B_{2}(z){\rm d}\epsilon_{2}

where
\frac{\partial A(z)}{\partial\epsilon_{1}}=\frac{\partial A(z)}{\partial\epsilon_{2}}=0

and

\displaystyle A(z)=\sum_{k=0}^{r-1}\sum_{l=0}^{m-1}a_{k,l}z^{l}\tilde{N}^{k}+z^{m-1}R_{r}+z^{3m-1}A^{\prime}(z),

(10.10)

\displaystyle B_{1}(z)=\sum_{k=0}^{r-1}\sum_{l=0}^{m-2}\frac{rb_{1,k,l}}{(-mr+lr+r+k)z^{m-l-1}}\tilde{N}^{k},

\displaystyle B_{2}(z)=\sum_{k=0}^{r-1}\sum_{l=0}^{m-2}\frac{rb^{(i)}_{2,k,l}}{(-mr+lr+r+k)z^{m-l-1}}\tilde{N}^{k},

\displaystyle C_{1}(z)\frac{{\rm d}z}{z^{m}}={\rm d}B_{1}(z)+[A(z),B_{1}(z)]\frac{{\rm d}z}{z^{m}},\qquad C_{2}(z)\frac{{\rm d}z}{z^{m}}={\rm d}B_{2}(z)+[A(z),B_{2}(z)]\frac{{\rm d}z}{z^{m}}.

Then we can see by the above equality that

C_{j}(z)|_{2m\tilde{x}}=\sum_{k=0}^{r-1}\sum_{l=0}^{m-2}b_{j,k,l}z^{l}\tilde{N}^{k}|_{2m\tilde{x}}

for
j=1,2
.
So we have

[C_{1}(z),B_{2}(z)],[C_{2}(z),B_{1}(z)]\in z^{m+1}\mathop{\rm End}\nolimits(\tilde{E}|_{U_{\alpha}})
.

Claim 10.11
.

[C_{1}(z),B_{2}(z)]=[C_{2}(z),B_{1}(z)]
.

Proof.

First notice that we can check the equality

{\rm d}B_{1}(z)=\sum_{k=0}^{r-1}\sum_{l=0}^{m-2}\frac{r(-m+l+1)+k}{-mr+lr+r+k}\frac{b_{1,k,l}}{z^{m-l}}\tilde{N}^{k}{\rm d}z+\left[B_{1}(z),R_{r}\right]\frac{{\rm d}z}{z}

using (
10.6
).
So we have

\displaystyle[{\rm d}B_{1}(z),B_{2}(z)]

\displaystyle=\left[\left[B_{1}(z),R_{r}\right],B_{2}(z)\right]\frac{{\rm d}z}{z}

\displaystyle=\left[\left[B_{2}(z),R_{r}\right],B_{1}(z)\right]\frac{{\rm d}z}{z}+\left[[B_{1}(z),B_{2}(z)],R_{r}\right]\frac{{\rm d}z}{z}

\displaystyle=[{\rm d}B_{2}(z),B_{1}(z)],

because

[B_{1}(z),B_{2}(z)]=0
.
Thus we have

\displaystyle\left[C_{1}(z)\frac{{\rm d}z}{z^{m}},B_{2}(z)\right]

\displaystyle=\left[{\rm d}B_{1}(z)+[A(z),B_{1}(z)]\frac{{\rm d}z}{z^{m}},B_{2}(z)\right]

\displaystyle=[{\rm d}B_{1}(z),B_{2}(z)]+[[A(z),B_{1}(z)],B_{2}(z)]\frac{{\rm d}z}{z^{m}}

\displaystyle=[{\rm d}B_{2}(z),B_{1}(z)]+[[A(z),B_{2}(z)],B_{1}(z)]\frac{{\rm d}z}{z^{m}}=\left[C_{2}(z)\frac{{\rm d}z}{z^{m}},B_{1}(z)\right].\!\!\!

∎

We put

\displaystyle\tilde{A}(z):=\sum_{k=0}^{r-1}\sum_{l=0}^{m-1}a_{k,l}z^{l}\tilde{N}^{k}+z^{m-1}R_{r}+z^{3m-1}\tilde{A}^{\prime}(z),

\displaystyle B_{1,2}(z):=\sum_{k=0}^{r-1}\sum_{l=0}^{m-2}\frac{rb_{1,2,k,l}}{(-mr+lr+r+k)z^{m-l-1}}\tilde{N}^{k},

\displaystyle C_{1,2}(z)\frac{{\rm d}z}{z^{m}}:={\rm d}B_{1,2}(z)+\left([A(z),B_{1,2}(z)]+[C_{1}(z),B_{2}(z)]\right)\frac{{\rm d}z}{z^{m}},

where

\tilde{A}^{\prime}(z)
is a lift of
A^{\prime}(z)

as a matrix with coefficients in

{\mathcal{O}}_{U_{\alpha}[u]}

such that

\frac{\partial\tilde{A}(z)}{\partial\epsilon_{1}}=\frac{\partial\tilde{A}(z)}{\partial\epsilon_{2}}=0
.
Define a connection

\nabla^{u}_{\alpha}\colon{\mathcal{O}}_{U_{\alpha}[u]}^{\oplus r}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{O}}_{U_{\alpha}[u]}^{\oplus r}\otimes\Omega^{1}_{{\mathcal{C}}_{\tilde{M}^{\prime}[u]/\tilde{M}^{\prime}}}({\mathcal{D}}_{\tilde{M}^{\prime}[u]})

by setting

\nabla^{u}_{\alpha}={\rm d}+\big(\tilde{A}+\epsilon_{1}C_{1}+\epsilon_{2}C_{2}+\epsilon_{1}\epsilon_{2}C_{1,2}\big)\frac{{\rm d}z}{z^{m}}+B_{1}{\rm d}\epsilon_{1}+B_{2}{\rm d}\epsilon_{2}+B_{1,2}(\epsilon_{1}{\rm d}\epsilon_{2}+\epsilon_{2}{\rm d}\epsilon_{1}).

Then
\nabla^{u}_{\alpha}
is an integrable connection
because its curvature form becomes

\displaystyle(C_{1}+\epsilon_{2}C_{1,2}){\rm d}\epsilon_{1}\wedge\frac{{\rm d}z}{z^{m}}+(C_{2}+\epsilon_{1}C_{1,2}){\rm d}\epsilon_{2}\wedge\frac{{\rm d}z}{z^{m}}+({\rm d}B_{1}+\epsilon_{2}{\rm d}B_{1,2})\wedge{\rm d}\epsilon_{1}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad{}+({\rm d}B_{2}+\epsilon_{1}{\rm d}B_{1,2})\wedge{\rm d}\epsilon_{2}+B_{1,2}{\rm d}\epsilon_{1}\wedge{\rm d}\epsilon_{2}+B_{1,2}{\rm d}\epsilon_{2}\wedge{\rm d}\epsilon_{1}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad{}+[A,B_{1}+\epsilon_{2}B_{1,2}]\frac{{\rm d}z}{z^{m_{i}}}\wedge{\rm d}\epsilon_{1}+[A,B_{2}+\epsilon_{1}B_{1,2}]\frac{{\rm d}z}{z^{m_{i}}}\wedge{\rm d}\epsilon_{2}+\epsilon_{2}[C_{2},B_{1}]\frac{{\rm d}z}{z^{m}}\wedge{\rm d}\epsilon_{1}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad{}+\epsilon_{1}[C_{1},B_{2}]\frac{{\rm d}z}{z^{m}}\wedge{\rm d}\epsilon_{2}+[B_{1},B_{2}]{\rm d}\epsilon_{1}\wedge{\rm d}\epsilon_{2}

\displaystyle\qquad{}=\left({\rm d}B_{1}+(-C_{1}+[A,B_{1}])\frac{{\rm d}z}{z^{m}}\right)\wedge{\rm d}\epsilon_{1}+\left({\rm d}B_{2}+(-C_{2}+[A,B_{2}])\frac{{\rm d}z}{z^{m}}\right)\wedge{\rm d}\epsilon_{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad{}+\epsilon_{2}\left({\rm d}B_{1,2}+(-C_{1,2}+[A,B_{1,2}]+[C_{2},B_{1}])\frac{{\rm d}z}{z^{m}}\right)\wedge{\rm d}\epsilon_{1}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad{}+\epsilon_{1}\left({\rm d}B_{1,2}+(-C_{1,2}+[A,B_{1,2}]+[C_{1},B_{2}])\frac{{\rm d}z}{z^{m}}\right)\wedge{\rm d}\epsilon_{2}=0.

We can define

V^{u}_{k,\alpha}
,
\vartheta^{u}_{k,\alpha}
,
\varkappa^{u}_{k,\alpha}

on
{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{D}}[u]}^{\oplus r}

in the same way as in the proof of
Lemma
10.5
.
So we can get a local horizontal lift

\big({\mathcal{O}}_{U_{\alpha}[u]}^{\oplus r},\nabla^{u}_{\alpha},(V^{u}_{k,\alpha},\vartheta^{u}_{k,\alpha},\varkappa^{u}_{k,\alpha})_{0\leq k\leq r-1}\big)
,
which is a lift of the restriction

\big({\mathcal{E}}^{\bar{u}},\nabla^{\bar{u}},l^{\bar{u}},\ell^{\bar{u}},{\mathcal{V}}^{\bar{u}}\big)|_{U_{\alpha}[\bar{u}]}
.

Let

\big({\mathcal{O}}_{U_{\alpha}[u]}^{\oplus r},\nabla^{\prime}_{\alpha},(V^{\prime}_{k,\alpha},\vartheta^{\prime}_{k,\alpha},\varkappa^{\prime}_{k,\alpha})_{0\leq k\leq r-1}\big)

be another local horizontal lift with respect to
u
,
which is a lift of

\big({\mathcal{E}}^{\bar{u}},\nabla^{\bar{u}},l^{\bar{u}},\ell^{\bar{u}},{\mathcal{V}}^{\bar{u}}\big)|_{U_{\alpha}[\bar{u}]}
.
Then we can write

\nabla^{\prime}_{\alpha}={\rm d}+\big(\tilde{A}+\epsilon_{1}C_{1}+\epsilon_{2}C_{2}+\epsilon_{1}\epsilon_{2}C^{\prime}_{1,2}\big)\frac{{\rm d}z}{z^{m}}+B_{1}{\rm d}\epsilon_{1}+B_{2}{\rm d}\epsilon_{2}+B^{\prime}_{1,2}\epsilon_{1}{\rm d}\epsilon_{2}+B^{\prime}_{2,1}\epsilon_{2}{\rm d}\epsilon_{1}.

The integrability condition of
\nabla^{\prime}
implies the equalities

\displaystyle C^{\prime}_{1,2}(z)\frac{{\rm d}z}{z^{m}}

\displaystyle={\rm d}B^{\prime}_{1,2}(z)+\big([A(z),B^{\prime}_{1,2}(z)]+[C_{1}(z),B_{2}(z)]\big)\frac{{\rm d}z}{z^{m}}

\displaystyle={\rm d}B^{\prime}_{2,1}(z)+\big([A(z),B^{\prime}_{2,1}(z)]+[C_{1}(z),B_{2}(z)]\big)\frac{{\rm d}z}{z^{m}}

(10.11)

and
B^{\prime}_{1,2}=B^{\prime}_{2,1}
.
Since
\nabla^{\prime}
has the property of local horizontal lift,
we have

C^{\prime}_{1,2}(z)\big|_{m\tilde{x}}=\sum_{k=0}^{r-1}\sum_{l=0}^{m-2}b_{1,2,k,l}z^{l}\big(\tilde{N}\big)^{k}\big|_{m\tilde{x}}.

We can see that

[A(z),B^{\prime}_{1,2}(z)]
is regular from the equality
(
10.11
).
Since
A(z)
satisfies (
10.10
),
we can first verify

z^{m-1}B^{\prime}_{1,2}(z)\in{\mathcal{O}}_{(m-1)\tilde{x}}\big[\tilde{N}|_{(m-1)\tilde{x}}\big]
.
Combining with the condition (c) of
Definition
10.1
(iv),
we can take
\beta_{k,l}
such that

B^{\prime}_{1,2}(z)-\sum_{k=0}^{r-1}\sum_{l=0}^{m-1}\beta_{k,l}z^{l-m-1}\tilde{N}^{k}\in\mathop{\rm End}\nolimits\big(\tilde{E}|_{U_{\alpha}}\big).

is a matrix of regular functions whose constant term
is a lower triangular matrix.
Using the same argument as in the proof of
Lemma
10.6
,
we can see

\beta_{k,l}=\frac{rb_{1,2,k,l}}{-mr+l+r+k}.

So

B_{12}(z)-B^{\prime}_{12}(z)
becomes a matrix of regular functions
and

I_{r}+\epsilon_{1}\epsilon_{2}(B_{12}(z)-B^{\prime}_{12}(z))

gives an automorphism of

{\mathcal{O}}_{U_{\alpha}[u]}^{\oplus r}

which transform
\nabla^{\prime}_{\alpha}
to
\nabla^{u}_{\alpha}

and which sends
V^{\prime}_{k,\alpha}
to
V^{u}_{k,\alpha}
.
Furthermore, we can see that such a transform is uniquely
determined by the coefficient of
\epsilon_{2}\,d\epsilon_{1}
.
Thus the existence and the uniqueness of a ramified local horizontal lift
with respect to
u
is proved.

Patching the local horizontal lifts together,
we get a unique horizontal lift

\big({\mathcal{E}}^{\bar{u}},\nabla^{\bar{u}},l^{\bar{u}},\ell^{\bar{u}},{\mathcal{V}}^{\bar{u}}\big)

of

\big(\tilde{E},\tilde{\nabla},\tilde{l},\tilde{\ell},\tilde{\mathcal{V}}\big)_{\tilde{M}^{\prime}}

on

{\mathcal{C}}\times_{\mathcal{T}}\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits{\mathcal{O}}_{\tilde{M}^{\prime}}[\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}]/\big(\epsilon_{1}^{2},\epsilon_{2}^{2}\big)

with respect to
u
.
∎

11
Global generalized isomonodromic deformation

Definition 11.1
.

For each vector field
v\in T_{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}
,
the relative connection

\big({\mathcal{E}}^{v},\overline{\nabla^{v}},l^{v},\ell^{v},{\mathcal{V}}^{v}\big)

induced by the global horizontal lift

\big({\mathcal{E}}^{v},\nabla^{v},l^{v},\ell^{v},{\mathcal{V}}^{v}\big)

(which exists by Proposition
10.7
)
defines a morphism

I_{\Phi(v)}\colon\ \tilde{M}^{\prime}\times\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits\mathbb{C}[\epsilon]/\big(\epsilon^{2}\big)\longrightarrow\tilde{M}^{\prime}

which makes the diagram

\begin{CD}\tilde{M}^{\prime}\times\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits\mathbb{C}[\epsilon]/\big(\epsilon^{2}\big)@>{I_{\Phi(v)}}>{}>\tilde{M}^{\prime}\\
@V{\pi_{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}\times\mathrm{id}}V{}V@V{}V{\pi_{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}}V\\
{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime}\times\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits\mathbb{C}[\epsilon]@>{I_{v}}>{}>{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime}\end{CD}

(11.1)

commutative.
We can see by the uniqueness of the horizontal lift that
the morphism
I_{\Phi(v)}
descends to a morphism

M^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}_{{\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{D}}}(\lambda,\tilde{\mu},\tilde{\nu})_{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}\times\mathbb{C}[\epsilon]\longrightarrow M^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}_{{\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{D}}}(\lambda,\tilde{\mu},\tilde{\nu})_{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}

which corresponds to a vector field

\Phi(v)\in H^{0}\big(M^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}_{{\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{D}}}(\lambda,\tilde{\mu},\tilde{\nu})_{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}},T_{M^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}_{{\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{D}}}(\lambda,\tilde{\mu},\tilde{\nu})_{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}}\big).

We call this vector field
\Phi(v)

a generalized isomonodromic vector field.

Proposition 11.2
.

The map

\Phi\colon\ H^{0}({\mathcal{T}^{\prime}},T_{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}})\ni v\mapsto\Phi(v)\in H^{0}\big(M^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}_{{\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{D}}}(\lambda,\tilde{\mu},\tilde{\nu})_{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}},T_{M^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}_{{\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{D}}}(\lambda,\tilde{\mu},\tilde{\nu})_{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}}\big)

is a homomorphism of

H^{0}({\mathcal{T}^{\prime}},{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}})
-modules.

Proof.

Take vector fields

v_{1},v_{2}\in H^{0}({\mathcal{T}^{\prime}},T_{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}})
.
Then
(v_{1},v_{2})
corresponds to a morphism

\bar{u}\colon\ {\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}\times\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits\mathbb{C}[\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}]/\big(\epsilon_{1}^{2},\epsilon_{1}\epsilon_{2},\epsilon_{2}^{2}\big)\longrightarrow{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}

such that the composition

{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}\times\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits\mathbb{C}[\epsilon_{i}]/\big(\epsilon_{i}^{2}\big)\hookrightarrow{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}\times\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits\mathbb{C}[\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}]/\big(\epsilon_{1}^{2},\epsilon_{1}\epsilon_{2},\epsilon_{2}^{2}\big)\xrightarrow{\bar{u}}{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}

coincides with the morphism
I_{v_{i}}

for
i=1,2
.
Let

\Delta_{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}\colon\ {\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}\times\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits\mathbb{C}[\epsilon]/\big(\epsilon^{2}\big)\longrightarrow{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}\times\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits\mathbb{C}[\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}]/\big(\epsilon_{1}^{2},\epsilon_{1}\epsilon_{2},\epsilon_{2}^{2}\big)

be the morphism corresponding to the ring homomorphism

{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}[\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}]/\big(\epsilon_{1}^{2},\epsilon_{1}\epsilon_{2},\epsilon_{2}^{2}\big)\ni a+b_{1}\epsilon_{1}+b_{2}\epsilon_{2}\mapsto a+b_{1}\epsilon+b_{2}\epsilon\in{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}[\epsilon]/\big(\epsilon^{2}\big).

Then the composition

\bar{u}\circ\Delta_{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}\colon\ {\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}\times\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits\mathbb{C}[\epsilon]/\big(\epsilon^{2}\big)\xrightarrow{\Delta_{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}}{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}\times\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits\mathbb{C}[\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}]/\big(\epsilon_{1}^{2},\epsilon_{1}\epsilon_{2},\epsilon_{2}^{2}\big)\xrightarrow{\bar{u}}{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}

coincides with the morphism

I_{v_{1}+v_{2}}
corresponding to the vector field
v_{1}+v_{2}
.
By virtue of Proposition
10.9
,
there exists a horizontal lift

\big({\mathcal{E}}^{\bar{u}},\nabla^{\bar{u}},l^{\bar{u}},\ell^{\bar{u}},{\mathcal{V}}^{\bar{u}}\big)

of

\big(\tilde{E},\tilde{\nabla},\tilde{l},\tilde{\ell},\tilde{\mathcal{V}}\big)_{\tilde{M}^{\prime}}

with respect to

\bar{u}
.
By the same procedure as Definition
11.1
,
the flat family of connections induced by the horizontal lift

\big({\mathcal{E}}^{\bar{u}},\nabla^{\bar{u}},l^{\bar{u}},\ell^{\bar{u}},{\mathcal{V}}^{\bar{u}}\big)

provides a morphism

I_{\Phi(\bar{u})}\colon\tilde{M^{\prime}}\times\mathbb{C}[\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}]/\big(\epsilon_{1}^{2},\epsilon_{1}\epsilon_{2},\epsilon_{2}^{2}\big)\longrightarrow\tilde{M^{\prime}}

such that the right square of the diagram

\begin{CD}\tilde{M}^{\prime}\times\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits\mathbb{C}[\epsilon]/\big(\epsilon^{2}\big)@>{\Delta_{\tilde{M^{\prime}}}}>{}>\tilde{M}^{\prime}\times\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits\mathbb{C}[\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}]/\big(\epsilon_{1}^{2},\epsilon_{1}\epsilon_{2},\epsilon_{2}^{2}\big)@>{I_{\Phi(\bar{u})}}>{}>\tilde{M^{\prime}}\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}\times\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits\mathbb{C}[\epsilon]/\big(\epsilon^{2}\big)@>{{\Delta_{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}}}>{}>{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}\times\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits\mathbb{C}[\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}]/\big(\epsilon_{1}^{2},\epsilon_{1}\epsilon_{2},\epsilon_{2}^{2}\big)@>{\bar{u}}>{}>{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}\end{CD}

is commutative.
The left square of the above diagram is defined as a Cartesian diagram.
By the definition of horizontal lift, the pullback

\Delta_{\tilde{M}^{\prime}}^{*}\big({\mathcal{E}}^{\bar{u}},\nabla^{\bar{u}},l^{\bar{u}},\ell^{\bar{u}},{\mathcal{V}}^{\bar{u}}\big)

is a horizontal lift of

\big(\tilde{E},\tilde{\nabla},\tilde{l},\tilde{\ell},\tilde{\mathcal{V}}\big)_{\tilde{M}^{\prime}}

with respect to
I_{v_{1}+v_{2}}
.
So the composition

I_{\Phi(\bar{u})}\circ\Delta_{\tilde{M^{\prime}}}

coincides with the morphism

I_{\Phi(v_{1}+v_{2})}
determined by the vector field

\Phi(v_{1}+v_{2})
.
On the other hand, the morphism
I_{\Phi(\bar{u})}
corresponds to the pair

(\Phi(v_{1}),\Phi(v_{2}))
of vector fields and
the composition

I_{\Phi(\bar{u})}\circ\Delta_{\tilde{M^{\prime}}}
corresponds to
the vector field
\Phi(v_{1})+\Phi(v_{2})
.
So we have the equality

I_{\Phi(v_{1}+v_{2})}=I_{\Phi(v_{1})+\Phi(v_{2})}

which means the equality
\Phi(v_{1}+v_{2})=\Phi(v_{1})+\Phi(v_{2})
.

Take a vector field
v\in H^{0}({\mathcal{T}^{\prime}},T_{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}})

and a regular function

f\in H^{0}({\mathcal{T}^{\prime}},{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}})
.
Consider the morphism

\alpha_{f}\colon\ {\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}\times\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits\mathbb{C}[\epsilon]/\big(\epsilon^{2}\big)\longrightarrow{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}\times\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits\mathbb{C}[\epsilon]/\big(\epsilon^{2}\big)

corresponding to the ring homomorphism

{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}[\epsilon]/\big(\epsilon^{2}\big)\ni a+\epsilon b\mapsto a+\epsilon fb\in{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}[\epsilon]/\big(\epsilon^{2}\big).

Then the composition

{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}\times\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits\mathbb{C}[\epsilon]/\big(\epsilon^{2}\big)\xrightarrow{\alpha_{f}}{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}\times\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits\mathbb{C}[\epsilon]/\big(\epsilon^{2}\big)\xrightarrow{I_{v}}{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}

coincides with the morphism
I_{fv}

corresponding to the vector field
fv
.
As in Definition
11.1
,
the horizontal lift

\big({\mathcal{E}}^{v},\nabla^{v},l^{v},\ell^{v},{\mathcal{V}}^{v}\big)

induces a morphism

I_{\Phi(v)}\colon\tilde{M}^{\prime}\times\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits\mathbb{C}[\epsilon]/\big(\epsilon^{2}\big)\longrightarrow\tilde{M^{\prime}}

which makes the diagram

\begin{CD}\tilde{M^{\prime}}\times\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits\mathbb{C}[\epsilon]/\big(\epsilon^{2}\big)@>{(\alpha_{f})_{\tilde{M^{\prime}}}}>{}>\tilde{M^{\prime}}\times\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits\mathbb{C}[\epsilon]/\big(\epsilon^{2}\big)@>{I_{\Phi(v)}}>{}>\tilde{M^{\prime}}\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}\times\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits\mathbb{C}[\epsilon]/\big(\epsilon^{2}\big)@>{\alpha_{f}}>{}>{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}\times\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits\mathbb{C}[\epsilon]/\big(\epsilon^{2}\big)@>{I_{v}}>{}>{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}\end{CD}

commutative, where the right square is Cartesian.
By the definition of horizontal lift,
the pullback

(\alpha_{f})_{\tilde{M^{\prime}}}^{*}\big({\mathcal{E}}^{v},\nabla^{v},l^{v},\ell^{v},{\mathcal{V}}^{v}\big)

is a horizontal lift of

\big(\tilde{E},\tilde{\nabla},\tilde{l},\tilde{\ell},\tilde{\mathcal{V}}\big)_{\tilde{M}^{\prime}}

with respect to
fv
.
So the composition

I_{\Phi(v)}\circ(\alpha_{f})_{\tilde{M^{\prime}}}

coincides with the morphism

I_{\Phi(fv)}
corresponding to
\Phi(fv)
.
On the other hand,
the composition

I_{\Phi(v)}\circ(\alpha_{f})_{\tilde{M^{\prime}}}

coincides with the morphism

I_{f\Phi(v)}
corresponding to the vector field

f\Phi(v)
.
So we have

\Phi(fv)=f\Phi(v)
.
∎

By Proposition
11.2
,

\Phi
defines a homomorphism

\Phi\colon\ T_{\mathcal{T}}\longrightarrow(\pi_{\mathcal{T}})_{*}T_{M^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}_{{\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{D}}}(\lambda,\tilde{\mu},\tilde{\nu})}

of sheaves of
{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{T}}
-modules.
By the adjoint property,
\Phi
corresponds to a
homomorphism

\Psi\colon\ (\pi_{\mathcal{T}})^{*}T_{\mathcal{T}}\longrightarrow T_{M^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}_{{\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{D}}}(\lambda,\tilde{\mu},\tilde{\nu})}

(11.2)

Since the diagram (
11.1
)
in Definition
11.1
is commutative,
we can see

{\rm d}\pi_{\mathcal{T}}\circ\Psi=\mathrm{id}_{T_{\mathcal{T}}}

for the canonical surjection

{\rm d}\pi_{\mathcal{T}}\colon T_{M^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}_{{\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{D}}}(\lambda,\tilde{\mu},\tilde{\nu})}\longrightarrow(\pi_{\mathcal{T}})^{*}T_{\mathcal{T}}
.
In particular,
the image

\mathop{\rm Im}\nolimits\Psi
is a subbundle of

T_{M^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}_{{\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{D}}}(\lambda,\tilde{\mu},\tilde{\nu})}
.

Definition 11.3
.

We call
\mathop{\rm Im}\nolimits\Psi
the
generalized isomonodromic subbundle of

T_{M^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}_{{\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{D}}}(\lambda,\tilde{\mu},\tilde{\nu})}
.

By using the generalized isomonodromic subbundle
\mathop{\rm Im}\nolimits\Psi
,
we can extend the relative symplectic form

\omega_{M^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}_{{\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{D}}}(\lambda,\tilde{\mu},\tilde{\nu})}

constructed in Theorem
8.1

to a total
2
-form on the moduli space

M^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}_{{\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{D}}}(\lambda,\tilde{\mu},\tilde{\nu})

in the following.

Definition 11.4
.

We define a
2
-form

\omega_{M^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}_{{\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{D}}}(\lambda,\tilde{\mu},\tilde{\nu})}^{\mathrm{GIM}}

on
M^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}_{{\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{D}}}(\lambda,\tilde{\mu},\tilde{\nu})

by setting

\omega_{M^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}_{{\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{D}}}(\lambda,\tilde{\mu},\tilde{\nu})}^{\mathrm{GIM}}(v_{1},v_{2})=\omega_{M^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}_{{\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{D}}}(\lambda,\tilde{\mu},\tilde{\nu})}\big(v_{1}-\Psi({\rm d}\pi_{\mathcal{T}}(v_{1})),v_{2}-\Psi({\rm d}\pi_{\mathcal{T}}(v_{2}))\big)

for
v_{1},v_{2}\in T_{M^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}_{{\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{D}}}(\lambda,\tilde{\mu},\tilde{\nu})}

and call it the generalized isomonodromic
2
-form.

Remark 11.5
.

In the logarithmic case,
the above formulation of isomonodromic
2
-form is given by
A. Komyo in
[
18
]
.
For a vector field
v\in T_{M^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}_{{\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{D}}}(\lambda,\tilde{\mu},\tilde{\nu})}

we can immediately see the equivalence

v\in\mathop{\rm Im}\nolimits\Psi\Leftrightarrow\omega_{M^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}_{{\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{D}}}(\lambda,\tilde{\mu},\tilde{\nu})}^{\mathrm{GIM}}(v,w)=0\qquad\text{for any $w\in T_{M^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}_{{\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{D}}}(\lambda,\tilde{\mu},\tilde{\nu})}$}

from the definition of the generalized isomonodromic
2
-form.
So the generalized isomonodromic
2
-form recovers the generalized isomonodromic
subbundle.

Theorem 11.6
.

For any vector fields
v_{1},v_{2}\in T_{\mathcal{T}}
,
the equality

\Phi([v_{1},v_{2}])=[\Phi(v_{1}),\Phi(v_{2})]

holds, where
[v_{1},v_{2}]=v_{1}v_{2}-v_{2}v_{1}
is the commutator of the vector fields
v_{1}
,
v_{2}
.
In particular,
the generalized isomonodromic subbundle
\mathop{\rm Im}\nolimits\Psi
of

T_{M^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}_{{\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{D}}}(\lambda,\tilde{\mu},\tilde{\nu})}

satisfies the integrability condition

[\mathop{\rm Im}\nolimits\Psi,\mathop{\rm Im}\nolimits\Psi]\subset\mathop{\rm Im}\nolimits\Psi.

Proof.

Take vector fields

v_{1},v_{2}\in H^{0}({\mathcal{T}^{\prime}},T_{\mathcal{T}}|_{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}})

over a Zariski open subset
{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}
of
{\mathcal{T}}
.
Let

\tilde{I}_{v_{1}}\colon\ {\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}\times\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits\mathbb{C}[\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}]/\big(\epsilon_{1}^{2},\epsilon_{2}^{2}\big)\longrightarrow{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}\times\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits\mathbb{C}[\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}]/\big(\epsilon_{1}^{2},\epsilon_{2}^{2}\big)

be the automorphism corresponding to the ring automorphism
\tilde{I}_{v_{1}}^{*}
of

{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}[\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}]/\big(\epsilon_{1}^{2},\epsilon_{2}^{2}\big)

defined by

\tilde{I}_{v_{1}}^{*}\left(a+b_{1}\epsilon_{1}+b_{2}\epsilon_{2}+c\,\epsilon_{1}\epsilon_{2}\right)=a+(v_{1}(a)+b_{1})\epsilon_{1}+b_{2}\epsilon_{2}+(v_{1}(b_{2})+c)\epsilon_{1}\epsilon_{2}.

Similarly, we can define an automorphism

\tilde{I}_{v_{2}}
of

{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}\times\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits\mathbb{C}[\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}]/\big(\epsilon_{1}^{2},\epsilon_{2}^{2}\big)

corresponding to
v_{2}
.
By construction, we can see that

\tilde{I}_{-v_{1}}=\tilde{I}_{v_{1}}^{-1}
and
\tilde{I}_{-v_{2}}=\tilde{I}_{v_{2}}^{-1}
.
The composition

\tilde{I}_{v_{2}}\circ\tilde{I}_{v_{1}}\circ\tilde{I}_{-v_{2}}\circ\tilde{I}_{-v_{1}}

corresponds to the ring automorphism of

{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}[\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}]/\big(\epsilon_{1}^{2},\epsilon_{2}^{2}\big)

determined by

\displaystyle\tilde{I}_{-v_{1}}^{*}\circ\tilde{I}_{-v_{2}}^{*}\circ\tilde{I}_{v_{1}}^{*}\circ\tilde{I}_{v_{2}}^{*}(a+b_{1}\epsilon_{1}+b_{2}\epsilon_{2}+c\,\epsilon_{1}\epsilon_{2})

\displaystyle\quad{}=\tilde{I}_{-v_{1}}^{*}\circ\tilde{I}_{-v_{2}}^{*}\circ\tilde{I}_{v_{1}}^{*}(a+b_{1}\epsilon_{1}+(v_{2}(a)+b_{2})\epsilon_{2}+(c+v_{2}(b_{1}))\epsilon_{1}\epsilon_{2})

\displaystyle\quad{}=\tilde{I}_{-v_{1}}^{*}\circ\tilde{I}_{-v_{2}}^{*}(a+(v_{1}(a)+b_{1})\epsilon_{1}+(v_{2}(a)+b_{2})\epsilon_{2}+(v_{1}v_{2}(a)+v_{1}(b_{2})+c+v_{2}(b_{1}))\epsilon_{1}\epsilon_{2})

\displaystyle\quad{}=\tilde{I}_{-v_{1}}^{*}(a+(v_{1}(a)+b_{1})\epsilon_{1}+b_{2}\epsilon_{2}+(-v_{2}v_{1}(a)+v_{1}v_{2}(a)+v_{1}(b_{2})+c)\epsilon_{1}\epsilon_{2})

\displaystyle\quad{}=a+b_{1}\epsilon_{1}+b_{2}\epsilon_{2}+((v_{1}v_{2}-v_{2}v_{1})(a)+c)\epsilon_{1}\epsilon_{2}.

Let

\rho\colon\ {\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}\times\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits\mathbb{C}[\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}]/\big(\epsilon_{1}^{2},\epsilon_{2}^{2}\big)\longrightarrow{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}\times\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits\mathbb{C}[\epsilon]/\big(\epsilon^{2}\big)

be the morphism
corresponding to the ring homomorphism

\rho^{*}\colon{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}[\epsilon]/\big(\epsilon^{2}\big)\rightarrow{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}[\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}]/\big(\epsilon_{1}^{2},\epsilon_{2}^{2}\big)

determined by

\rho^{*}(a+c\epsilon)=a+c\epsilon_{1}\epsilon_{2}
.
Then the composition

{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}\times\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits\mathbb{C}[\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}]/\big(\epsilon_{1}^{2},\epsilon_{2}^{2}\big)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rho}}{{\longrightarrow}}{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}\times\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits\mathbb{C}[\epsilon]/\big(\epsilon^{2}\big)\xrightarrow{I_{v_{1}v_{2}-v_{2}v_{1}}}{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}

(11.3)

coincides with the composition

\displaystyle{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}\times\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits\mathbb{C}[\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}]/\big(\epsilon_{1}^{2},\epsilon_{2}^{2}\big)\xrightarrow{\tilde{I}_{v_{2}}\circ\tilde{I}_{v_{1}}\circ\tilde{I}_{v_{2}}^{-1}\circ\tilde{I}_{v_{1}}^{-1}}{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}\times\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits\mathbb{C}[\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}]/\big(\epsilon_{1}^{2},\epsilon_{2}^{2}\big)

\displaystyle\hphantom{{\mathcal{T}'}\times\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits\mathbb{C}[\epsilon_1,\epsilon_2]/\big(\epsilon_1^2,\epsilon_2^2\big)
\xrightarrow{ \tilde{I}_{v_{2}}\circ\tilde{I}_{v_{1}}\circ\tilde{I}_{v_{2}}^{-1}\circ\tilde{I}_{v_{1}}^{-1} }}{}\xrightarrow{\text{trivial projection}}{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}.

(11.4)

By Proposition
10.9
,
there exists a horizontal lift

\big({\mathcal{E}}^{\tilde{v}_{i}},\nabla^{\tilde{v}_{i}},l^{\tilde{v}_{i}},\ell^{\tilde{v}_{i}},{\mathcal{V}}^{\tilde{v}_{i}}\big)

of
\big(\tilde{E},\tilde{\nabla},\tilde{l},\tilde{\ell},\tilde{\mathcal{V}}\big)_{\tilde{M}^{\prime}}

with respect to the morphism

{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}\times\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits\mathbb{C}[\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}]/\big(\epsilon_{1}^{2},\epsilon_{2}^{2}\big)\xrightarrow{\tilde{I}_{v_{i}}}{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}\times\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits\mathbb{C}[\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}]/\big(\epsilon_{1}^{2},\epsilon_{2}^{2}\big)\xrightarrow{\text{trivial projection}}{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}.

For the relative connection

\overline{\nabla^{\tilde{v}_{i}}}
induced by
\nabla^{\tilde{v}_{i}}
,
the flat family

\big({\mathcal{E}}^{\tilde{v}_{i}},\overline{\nabla^{\tilde{v}_{i}}},l^{\tilde{v}_{i}},\ell^{\tilde{v}_{i}},{\mathcal{V}}^{\tilde{v}_{i}}\big)

determines a morphism

I_{\Phi(\tilde{v}_{i})}\colon\tilde{M}^{\prime}\times\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits\mathbb{C}[\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}]/\big(\epsilon_{1}^{2},\epsilon_{2}^{2}\big)\longrightarrow\tilde{M}^{\prime}

which is canonically extended to a morphism

\tilde{I}_{\Phi(\tilde{v}_{i})}\colon\ \tilde{M}^{\prime}\times\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits\mathbb{C}[\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}]/\big(\epsilon_{1}^{2},\epsilon_{2}^{2}\big)\longrightarrow\tilde{M}^{\prime}\times\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits\mathbb{C}[\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}]/\big(\epsilon_{1}^{2},\epsilon_{2}^{2}\big)

over
\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits\mathbb{C}[\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}]/\big(\epsilon_{1}^{2},\epsilon_{2}^{2}\big)
.
Furthermore, the diagram

\begin{CD}\tilde{M}^{\prime}\times\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits\mathbb{C}[\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}]/\big(\epsilon_{1}^{2},\epsilon_{2}^{2}\big)@>{\tilde{I}_{\Phi(\tilde{v}_{2})}\circ\tilde{I}_{\Phi(\tilde{v}_{1})}\circ\tilde{I}_{\Phi(\tilde{v}_{2})}^{-1}\circ\tilde{I}_{\Phi(\tilde{v}_{1})}^{-1}}>{}>\tilde{M}^{\prime}\times\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits\mathbb{C}[\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}]/\big(\epsilon_{1}^{2},\epsilon_{2}^{2}\big)\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}\times\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits\mathbb{C}[\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}]/\big(\epsilon_{1}^{2},\epsilon_{2}^{2}\big)@>{\tilde{I}_{v_{2}}\circ\tilde{I}_{v_{1}}\circ\tilde{I}_{v_{2}}^{-1}\circ\tilde{I}_{v_{1}}^{-1}}>{}>{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}\times\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits\mathbb{C}[\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}]/\big(\epsilon_{1}^{2},\epsilon_{2}^{2}\big)\end{CD}

is commutative.

By the definition of horizontal lift,
we can see that the pullback

\big(\tilde{I}_{\Phi(\tilde{v}_{1})}^{-1}\big)^{*}\big(\tilde{I}_{\Phi(\tilde{v}_{2})}^{-1}\big)^{*}\tilde{I}_{\Phi(\tilde{v}_{1})}^{*}\big({\mathcal{E}}^{\tilde{v}_{2}},\nabla^{\tilde{v}_{2}},l^{\tilde{v}_{2}},\ell^{\tilde{v}_{2}},{\mathcal{V}}^{\tilde{v}_{2}}\big)

becomes a horizontal lift of

\big(\tilde{E},\tilde{\nabla},\tilde{l},\tilde{\ell},\tilde{\mathcal{V}}\big)_{\tilde{M}^{\prime}}

with respect to the morphism
(
11.4
).
On the other hand, there is a canonical commutative diagram

\begin{CD}\tilde{M^{\prime}}\times\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits\mathbb{C}[\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}]/\big(\epsilon_{1}^{2},\epsilon_{2}^{2}\big)@>{\rho_{\tilde{M^{\prime}}}}>{}>\tilde{M^{\prime}}\times\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits\mathbb{C}[\epsilon]/\big(\epsilon^{2}\big)@>{I_{\Phi(v_{1}v_{2}-v_{2}v_{1})}}>{}>\tilde{M^{\prime}}\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}\times\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits\mathbb{C}[\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}]/\big(\epsilon_{1}^{2},\epsilon_{2}^{2}\big)@>{\rho}>{}>{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}\times\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits\mathbb{C}[\epsilon]/\big(\epsilon^{2}\big)@>{I_{v_{1}v_{2}-v_{2}v_{1}}}>{}>{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}},\end{CD}

whose left square is Cartesian.
So we can see that the pullback

\rho_{\tilde{M^{\prime}}}^{*}\big({\mathcal{E}}^{v_{1}v_{2}-v_{2}v_{1}},\nabla^{v_{1}v_{2}-v_{2}v_{1}},l^{v_{1}v_{2}-v_{2}v_{1}},\ell^{v_{1}v_{2}-v_{2}v_{1}},{\mathcal{V}}^{v_{1}v_{2}-v_{2}v_{1}}\big)

becomes a horizontal lift of

\big(\tilde{E},\tilde{\nabla},\tilde{l},\tilde{\ell},\tilde{\mathcal{V}}\big)_{\tilde{M}^{\prime}}

with respect to the morphism
(
11.3
).
Since the morphism (
11.4
)
coincides with the morphism (
11.3
),
we can deduce an isomorphism

\displaystyle\big(\tilde{I}_{\Phi(\tilde{v}_{1})}^{-1}\big)^{*}\big(\tilde{I}_{\Phi(\tilde{v}_{2})}^{-1}\big)^{*}\tilde{I}_{\Phi(\tilde{v}_{1})}^{*}\big({\mathcal{E}}^{\tilde{v}_{2}},\nabla^{\tilde{v}_{2}},l^{\tilde{v}_{2}},\ell^{\tilde{v}_{2}},{\mathcal{V}}^{\tilde{v}_{2}}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad{}\cong\rho_{\tilde{M^{\prime}}}^{*}\big({\mathcal{E}}^{v_{1}v_{2}-v_{2}v_{1}},\nabla^{v_{1}v_{2}-v_{2}v_{1}},l^{v_{1}v_{2}-v_{2}v_{1}},\ell^{v_{1}v_{2}-v_{2}v_{1}},{\mathcal{V}}^{v_{1}v_{2}-v_{2}v_{1}}\big)

by the uniqueness of horizontal lift
proved in Proposition
10.9
.
Considering the induced morphism, we have

(\text{trivial projection})\circ\tilde{I}_{\Phi(\tilde{v}_{2})\circ\tilde{I}_{\Phi(\tilde{v}_{1})}\circ\tilde{I}_{\Phi(\tilde{v}_{2})}^{-1}\circ\tilde{I}_{\Phi(\tilde{v}_{1})}^{-1}}=I_{\Phi(v_{1}v_{2}-v_{2}v_{1})}\circ\rho_{\tilde{M^{\prime}}},

from which we get

\Phi(v_{1}v_{2}-v_{2}v_{1})=\Phi(v_{1})\Phi(v_{2})-\Phi(v_{2})\Phi(v_{1})
.
∎

Definition 11.7
.

Since the subbundle

\mathop{\rm Im}\nolimits\Psi\subset T_{M^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}_{{\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{D}}}(\lambda,\tilde{\mu},\tilde{\nu})}

satisfies the integrability condition by Theorem
11.6
,
it determines a foliation

{\mathcal{F}}^{\rm GIM}_{T_{M^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}_{{\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{D}}}(\lambda,\tilde{\mu},\tilde{\nu})}}

on the moduli space

M^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}_{{\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{D}}}(\lambda,\tilde{\mu},\tilde{\nu})
.
We call it the generalized isomonodromic foliation.

Take a point
t_{0}\in{\mathcal{T}}

and a point
y
of the fiber

M^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}_{{\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{D}}}(\lambda,\tilde{\mu},\tilde{\nu})_{t_{0}}

over
t_{0}
.
Then we can take an analytic open neighborhood

{\mathcal{M}^{\prime}}
of
y
in

M^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}_{{\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{D}}}(\lambda,\tilde{\mu},\tilde{\nu})

and an analytic open neighborhood
{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}
of
t_{0}
in
{\mathcal{T}}

together with an analytic isomorphism

{\mathcal{M}^{\prime}}\cong{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}_{t_{0}}\times{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}

(11.5)

such that the restriction
\pi_{\mathcal{T}}|_{\mathcal{M}^{\prime}}
of

\pi_{\mathcal{T}}\colon M^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}_{{\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{D}}}(\lambda,\tilde{\mu},\tilde{\nu})\longrightarrow{\mathcal{T}}

coincides with the second projection
and that the fibers
\{\{y^{\prime}\}\times{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}\}_{y^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{M}^{\prime}_{t_{0}}}}

over
{\mathcal{M}^{\prime}_{t_{0}}}
are leaves in

{\mathcal{F}}^{\rm GIM}_{M^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}_{{\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{D}}}(\lambda,\tilde{\mu},\tilde{\nu})}
.

Take a holomorphic system of coordinates

\theta=(\theta_{1},\dots,\theta_{N})
of
{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}
.
If we set

{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}[\partial\theta]:={\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}\times\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits\mathbb{C}[\epsilon_{1},\dots,\epsilon_{N}]\big/\big(\epsilon_{i}\epsilon_{j}\mid 1\leq i,j\leq N\big),

then the tuple

\partial\theta=(\partial/\partial\theta_{1},\dots,\partial/\partial\theta_{N})

of vector fields on
{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}

corresponds to a morphism

I_{\partial\theta}\colon\ {\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}[\partial\theta]\longrightarrow{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}},

whose restriction to
{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}\subset{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}[\partial\theta]
is the identity morphism.

By the same proof as Proposition
10.9
,
we can construct a horizontal lift

\big({\mathcal{E}}^{\partial\theta},\nabla^{\partial\theta},l^{\partial\theta},\ell^{\partial\theta},\allowbreak{\mathcal{V}}^{\partial\theta}\big)

of the universal family

(E,\nabla,l,\ell,{\mathcal{V}})

on
{\mathcal{C}}\times_{\mathcal{T}}{\mathcal{M}^{\prime}}

with respect to the morphism
I_{\partial\theta}
.
On a small open subset

U\subset{\mathcal{C}}\times_{\mathcal{T}}{\mathcal{M}^{\prime}}
,
we may assume

E|_{U}\cong{\mathcal{O}}_{U}^{\oplus r}
.
Then we can write

\nabla|_{U}={\rm d}+A{\rm d}z

where
z
is a holomorphic coordinate on
{\mathcal{C}}\times_{\mathcal{T}}{\mathcal{M}^{\prime}}

over
{\mathcal{M}^{\prime}}
and
A
is a matrix of meromorphic functions in
z
.
Let
U[\partial\theta]\subset{\mathcal{C}}\times_{\mathcal{T}}{\mathcal{M}^{\prime}}[\partial\theta]

be the open subscheme whose underlying set is
U
.
Then we have

{\mathcal{E}}|_{U[\partial\theta]}\cong{\mathcal{O}}_{U[\partial\theta]}^{\oplus r}

and we can write

\nabla^{\partial\theta}={\rm d}+A(\epsilon){\rm d}z+\sum_{j=1}^{N}B_{j}{\rm d}\epsilon_{j}
,
where
A(\epsilon)
is a lift of
A
.
By the integrability condition of
\nabla^{\partial\theta}
, we have the equality

\sum_{j=1}^{N}\frac{\partial A(\epsilon)}{\partial\epsilon_{j}}{\rm d}\epsilon_{j}\wedge{\rm d}z+\sum_{j=1}^{N}\frac{\partial B_{j}}{\partial z}{\rm d}z\wedge{\rm d}\epsilon_{j}+\sum_{j=1}^{N}[A,B_{j}]{\rm d}z\wedge{\rm d}\epsilon_{j}=0.

Take a holomorphic coordinate system

x_{1},\dots,x_{\delta}
of
{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}_{t_{0}}
.
With respect to the coordinate system

z,x_{1},\dots,x_{\delta},\theta_{1},\dots,\theta_{N}
,
the partial derivative

\partial/\partial\theta_{j}
coincides with the vector field

\Phi(\partial/\partial\theta_{j})

and the partial derivative
\partial A/\partial\theta_{j}

coincides with

\partial A(\epsilon)/\partial\epsilon_{j}
.
So the above integrability condition of
\nabla^{\partial\theta}
is the same as the
integrability condition

\sum_{j=1}^{N}\frac{\partial A}{\partial\theta_{j}}{\rm d}\theta_{j}\wedge{\rm d}z+\sum_{j=1}^{N}\frac{\partial B_{j}}{\partial z}{\rm d}z\wedge{\rm d}\theta_{j}+\sum_{j=1}^{N}[A,B_{j}]{\rm d}z\wedge{\rm d}\theta_{j}=0

of the connection

\nabla_{U}^{\rm flat}={\rm d}+A{\rm d}z+\sum_{j=1}^{N}B_{j}{\rm d}\theta_{j}

on
E|_{U}
relative to the composition

U\hookrightarrow{\mathcal{C}}\times_{\mathcal{T}}{\mathcal{M}^{\prime}}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{M}^{\prime}}\cong{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}_{t_{0}}\times{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}\xrightarrow{\pi_{{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}_{t_{0}}}}{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}_{t_{0}},

where

\pi_{{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}_{t_{0}}}\colon{\mathcal{M}^{\prime}}\cong{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}_{t_{0}}\times{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}_{t_{0}}

is the first projection.
So we can see from Theorem
9.7

and Corollary
9.11

that
\nabla|_{U}
is a local generalized isomonodromic deformation
in the sense of Definition
9.4

or Definition
9.10
.

We can patch
\nabla_{U}^{\rm flat}
together to get a global connection on
E
.
Indeed, take another open subset
U^{\prime}\subset{\mathcal{C}}\times_{\mathcal{T}}{\mathcal{M}^{\prime}}

and write
\nabla|_{U^{\prime}}={\rm d}+A^{\prime}{\rm d}z
.
Then we have

P^{-1}{\rm d}P+P^{-1}A{\rm d}zP=A^{\prime}{\rm d}z

for a transition matrix
P
.
There is a local horizontal lift
{\rm d}+A^{\prime}(\epsilon){\rm d}z+\sum_{j=1}^{N}B^{\prime}_{j}{\rm d}\epsilon_{j}

of
\nabla|_{U^{\prime}}

and by the uniqueness of the local horizontal lift,
we have a uniquely lift
P(\epsilon)
of
P

satisfying

\displaystyle P(\epsilon)^{-1}\left(\frac{\partial P(\epsilon)}{\partial z}{\rm d}z+\sum_{j=1}^{N}\frac{\partial P(\epsilon)}{\partial\epsilon_{j}}{\rm d}\epsilon_{j}\right)+P(\epsilon)^{-1}\left(A(\epsilon){\rm d}z+\sum_{j=1}^{N}B_{j}{\rm d}\epsilon_{j}\right)P(\epsilon)

\displaystyle\qquad{}=A^{\prime}(\epsilon){\rm d}z+\sum_{j=1}^{N}B^{\prime}_{j}{\rm d}\epsilon_{j}.

Since

\partial P(\epsilon)/\partial\epsilon_{j}=\partial P/\partial\theta_{j}
,
the above equality yields the equality

P^{-1}\left(\frac{\partial P}{\partial z}{\rm d}z+\sum_{j=1}^{N}\frac{\partial P}{\partial\theta_{j}}{\rm d}\theta_{j}\right)+P^{-1}\left(A{\rm d}z+\sum_{j=1}^{N}B_{j}{\rm d}\theta_{j}\right)P=A^{\prime}{\rm d}z+\sum_{j=1}^{N}B^{\prime}_{j}{\rm d}\theta_{j}.

So we can patch the local connections

\nabla^{\rm flat}_{U}
together
to get an integrable connection

\nabla^{\rm flat}\colon\ E\longrightarrow E\otimes\Omega_{{\mathcal{C}}\times_{\mathcal{T}}{\mathcal{M}^{\prime}}/{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}_{t_{0}}}(D_{\mathcal{M}^{\prime}})

(11.6)

relative to the composition

{\mathcal{C}}\times_{\mathcal{T}}{\mathcal{M}^{\prime}}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{M}^{\prime}}\cong{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}_{t_{0}}\times{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}_{t_{0}}
.

Corollary 11.8
.

The generalized isomonodromic
2
-form

\omega_{M^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}_{{\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{D}}}(\lambda,\tilde{\mu},\tilde{\nu})}^{\mathrm{GIM}}

constructed in Definition
11.4
is
{\rm d}
-closed.

Proof.

Under the above notations, we will prove the equality

\omega_{M^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}_{{\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{D}}}(\lambda,\tilde{\mu},\tilde{\nu})}^{\mathrm{GIM}}\big|_{{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}}=\pi_{{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}_{t_{0}}}^{*}\big(\omega_{M^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}_{{\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{D}}}(\lambda,\tilde{\mu},\tilde{\nu})}\big|_{{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}_{t_{0}}}\big)

(11.7)

where

\pi_{{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}_{t_{0}}}\colon{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}\cong{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}_{t_{0}}\times{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}_{t_{0}}

corresponds to the first projection with respect to the isomorphism
(
11.5
).
The corollary follows from this equality, since

\omega_{M^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}_{{\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{D}}}(\lambda,\tilde{\mu},\tilde{\nu})}|_{{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}_{t_{0}}}

is
d
-closed by Theorem
8.1
.

Take two tangent vectors

v,v^{\prime}\in T_{{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}}(y,t_{0})

at
(y,t)\in{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}_{t_{0}}\times{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}
.
We have the equalities

(\pi_{{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}_{t_{0}}})_{*}(v)=(\pi_{{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}_{t_{0}}})_{*}(v-\Phi(\pi_{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}})_{*}(v)),\qquad(\pi_{{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}_{t_{0}}})_{*}(v^{\prime})=(\pi_{{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}_{t_{0}}})_{*}(v-\Phi(\pi_{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}})_{*}(v^{\prime}))

because
\{y\times{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}\}_{y\in{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}_{t_{0}}}

are leaves of the foliation

{\mathcal{F}}^{\rm GIM}_{M^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}_{{\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{D}}}(\lambda,\tilde{\mu},\tilde{\nu})}
,
which is determined by the subbundle
\mathop{\rm Im}\nolimits\Phi

of
T_{M^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}_{{\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{D}}}(\lambda,\tilde{\mu},\tilde{\nu})}
.
The tangent vector

(\pi_{{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}_{t_{0}}})_{*}(v-\Phi(\pi_{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}})_{*}(v))

corresponds to
a morphism

\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits\mathbb{C}[\epsilon]/\big(\epsilon^{2}\big)\longrightarrow{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}_{t_{0}}
.
Let

\tilde{I}_{v}\colon\ \mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits\mathbb{C}[\epsilon]/\big(\epsilon^{2}\big)\times{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}_{t_{0}}\times{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}\cong{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}

be its base change.

We can construct a complex

{\mathcal{F}}^{\bullet}
of sheaves on
{\mathcal{C}}\times_{\mathcal{T}}{\mathcal{M}^{\prime}}

from the universal family

(E,\nabla,l,\ell,{\mathcal{V}})

in the same way as (
6.12
)
in Section
6
.
Since
(\mathrm{id}\times\tilde{I}_{v})^{*}(E,\nabla,l,\ell,{\mathcal{V}})

is a lift of
(E,\nabla,l,\ell,{\mathcal{V}})|_{{\mathcal{C}}_{y\times{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}}}
,
it induces a gluing data

\{u_{\alpha\beta},v_{\alpha},\eta_{\alpha}\}

with respect to an open covering
\{U_{\alpha}\}
of

{\mathcal{C}}_{y\times{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}}:={\mathcal{C}}\times_{\mathcal{T}}(y\times{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}})
.
as in Proposition
6.3
.
Set

\tilde{v}:=[\{u_{\alpha\beta},v_{\alpha},\eta_{\alpha}\}]\in\mathbb{R}^{1}(p_{y\times{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}})_{*}\big({\mathcal{F}}^{\bullet}|_{{\mathcal{C}}_{y\times{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}}}\big).

Then we can see from the construction of
\tilde{v}
that the equalities

\displaystyle\tilde{v}|_{(y,t_{0})}=(\pi_{{\mathcal{M}^{\prime}}_{t_{0}}})_{*}(v)\in\mathbb{H}^{1}\big({\mathcal{F}}^{\bullet}|_{{\mathcal{C}}_{(y,t_{0})}}\big),

\displaystyle\tilde{v}|_{(y,t)}=v-\Phi(\pi_{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}})_{*}(v)\in\mathbb{H}^{1}\big({\mathcal{F}}^{\bullet}|_{{\mathcal{C}}_{(y,t)}}\big).

hold.
We can similarly construct an element

\tilde{v^{\prime}}=[\{u^{\prime}_{\alpha\beta},v^{\prime}_{\alpha},\eta^{\prime}_{\alpha}\}]
of

\mathbb{R}^{1}(p_{y\times{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}})_{*}\big({\mathcal{F}}^{\bullet}|_{{\mathcal{C}}_{y\times{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}}}\big)

from the tangent vector
v^{\prime}
.
Recall the construction of the complex
{\mathcal{F}}^{\bullet}

in (
6.12
).
Since the map
{\mathcal{G}}^{1}\longrightarrow G^{1}
is surjective
and the map
{\mathcal{G}}^{0}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{S}}^{1}_{\rm ram}

is a surjection to the kernel of the surjection

{\mathcal{S}}^{1}_{\rm ram}\longrightarrow A^{1}
,
we can replace
u_{\alpha\beta}
,
v_{\alpha}
so that

\eta_{\alpha}=0
holds.
Similarly we may assume
\eta^{\prime}_{\alpha}=0
.

Consider the pairing

\omega\big(\tilde{v},\tilde{v^{\prime}}\big):=\big[\big\{\mathop{\rm Tr}\nolimits(u_{\alpha\beta}u^{\prime}_{\beta\gamma}),-\mathop{\rm Tr}\nolimits(u_{\alpha\beta}v_{\beta}-v_{\alpha}u^{\prime}_{\alpha\beta})\big\}\big]\in\mathbb{R}^{2}(p_{y\times{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}})_{*}\big(\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\mathcal{C}}_{y\times{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}}/{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}}\big)\cong{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}

of
\tilde{v}
and
\tilde{v^{\prime}}
.
Then we have the equalities

\displaystyle\pi_{{\mathcal{M}^{\prime}}_{t_{0}}}^{*}\big(\omega_{M^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}_{{\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{D}}}(\lambda,\tilde{\mu},\tilde{\nu})}|_{{\mathcal{M}^{\prime}}_{t_{0}}}\big)(v,v^{\prime})=\omega_{M^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}_{{\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{D}}}(\lambda,\tilde{\mu},\tilde{\nu})_{t_{0}}}\big((\pi_{{\mathcal{M}^{\prime}}_{t_{0}}})_{*}(v),(\pi_{{\mathcal{M}^{\prime}}_{t_{0}}})_{*}(v^{\prime})\big)=\omega\big(\tilde{v},\tilde{v^{\prime}}\big)|_{(y,t_{0})},

\displaystyle\omega^{\rm GIM}_{M^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}_{{\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{D}}}(\lambda,\tilde{\mu},\tilde{\nu})}(v,v^{\prime})=\omega_{M^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}_{{\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{D}}}(\lambda,\tilde{\mu},\tilde{\nu})_{t}}\big(v-\Phi(\pi_{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}})_{*}(v),v^{\prime}-\Phi(\pi_{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}})_{*}(v^{\prime})\big)=\omega\big(\tilde{v},\tilde{v^{\prime}}\big)|_{(y,t)}.

So, in order to prove (
11.7
),
we only have to prove that

\omega\big(\tilde{v},\tilde{v^{\prime}}\big)\in{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}

is constant on
{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}
.
We may assume that
{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime}
is isomorphic to a polydisk.
Then it is sufficient to show that

\omega\big(\tilde{v},\tilde{v^{\prime}}\big)

belongs to the image of the canonical map

\displaystyle\mathbb{C}\cong\mathbb{H}^{2}\big({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{C}}_{y\times{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime}}}\xrightarrow{{\rm d}}\Omega^{1}_{{\mathcal{C}}_{y\times{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime}}}\xrightarrow{{\rm d}}\cdots\xrightarrow{{\rm d}}\Omega^{N+1}_{{\mathcal{C}}_{y\times{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime}}}\big)

\displaystyle\hphantom{\mathbb{C}}{}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{R}^{2}(p_{y\times{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}})_{*}\big({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{C}}_{y\times{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime}}}\xrightarrow{{\rm d}}\Omega^{1}_{{\mathcal{C}}_{y\times{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime}}/{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}}\big)\cong{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}.

(11.8)

Recall that
(E,\nabla)
can be extended to the family of integrable connections

\big(E,\nabla^{\rm flat}\big)
in (
11.6
).
Then the pullback

\big(\mathrm{id}\times\tilde{I}_{v}\big)^{*}\big(E,\nabla^{\rm flat}\big)

is a family of integrable connections relative to
\mathop{\rm Spec}\nolimits\mathbb{C}[\epsilon]

whose induced relative connection is

\big(\mathrm{id}\times\tilde{I}_{v}\big)^{*}(E,\nabla)
.
So we can extend the relative meromorphic differential

v_{\alpha}=B_{\alpha}{\rm d}z
to a total differential

v^{\rm flat}_{\alpha}=B_{\alpha}{\rm d}z+\sum_{i=1}^{N}C^{i}_{\alpha}{\rm d}\theta_{i}

which satisfies the patching condition

(\mathrm{id}+\epsilon u_{\alpha\beta})\circ\big(\nabla^{\rm flat}+\epsilon v^{\rm flat}_{\beta}\big)=\big(\nabla^{\rm flat}+\epsilon v^{\rm flat}_{\alpha}\big)\circ(\mathrm{id}+\epsilon u_{\alpha\beta})

on
E|_{U_{\alpha\beta}}\otimes\mathbb{C}[\epsilon]

and the integrability condition

\big(\nabla^{\rm flat}+\epsilon v^{\rm flat}_{\alpha}\big)\circ\big(\nabla^{\rm flat}+\epsilon v^{\rm flat}_{\alpha}\big)=0.

Let

\displaystyle\nabla^{\rm flat}_{{\dagger}}\colon\ {\mathcal{E}}{\rm nd}(E_{y\times{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}})\ni u\mapsto\nabla^{\rm flat}\circ u-(u\otimes\mathrm{id})\circ\nabla^{\rm flat}\in{\mathcal{E}}{\rm nd}(E_{y\times{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}})\otimes\Omega^{1}_{{\mathcal{C}}\times_{\mathcal{T}}(y\times{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime})}(D_{y\times{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime}})

be the induced connection on
{\mathcal{E}}{\rm nd}(E|_{{\mathcal{C}}_{y\times{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}}})
.
Then the above two equalities become

\displaystyle\nabla^{\rm flat}_{{\dagger}}(u_{\alpha\beta})=v_{\beta}^{\rm flat}-v_{\alpha}^{\rm flat},\qquad\nabla^{\rm flat}_{{\dagger}}\big(v^{\rm flat}_{\alpha}\big)=0.

We can check the equalities

\displaystyle{\rm d}\mathop{\rm Tr}\nolimits\big(u_{\alpha\beta}u^{\prime}_{\beta\gamma}\big)=\mathop{\rm Tr}\nolimits\big(\nabla^{\rm flat}_{{\dagger}}(u_{\alpha\beta}u^{\prime}_{\beta\gamma})\big)=\mathop{\rm Tr}\nolimits\big(\nabla^{\rm flat}_{{\dagger}}(u_{\alpha\beta})u^{\prime}_{\beta\gamma}+u_{\alpha\beta}\nabla^{\rm flat}_{{\dagger}}(u^{\prime}_{\beta\gamma})\big)

\displaystyle\hphantom{{\rm d} \mathop{\rm Tr}\nolimits\big(u_{\alpha\beta}u'_{\beta\gamma}\big)}{}=\mathop{\rm Tr}\nolimits\big((v_{\beta}^{\rm flat}-v_{\alpha}^{\rm flat})u^{\prime}_{\beta\gamma}+u_{\alpha\beta}(v^{\prime\rm flat}_{\gamma}-v^{\prime\rm flat}_{\beta})\big)

\displaystyle\hphantom{{\rm d} \mathop{\rm Tr}\nolimits\big(u_{\alpha\beta}u'_{\beta\gamma}\big)}{}=\mathop{\rm Tr}\nolimits\big(v_{\beta}^{\rm flat}u^{\prime}_{\beta\gamma}-v_{\alpha}^{\rm flat}(u^{\prime}_{\alpha\gamma}-u^{\prime}_{\alpha\beta})+(u_{\alpha\gamma}-u_{\beta\gamma})v^{\prime\rm flat}_{\gamma}-u_{\alpha\beta}v^{\prime\rm flat}_{\beta}\big)

\displaystyle\hphantom{{\rm d} \mathop{\rm Tr}\nolimits\big(u_{\alpha\beta}u'_{\beta\gamma}\big)}{}=\mathop{\rm Tr}\nolimits\big({-}u_{\beta\gamma}v^{\prime\rm flat}_{\gamma}+v^{\rm flat}_{\beta}u^{\prime}_{\beta\gamma}\big)-\mathop{\rm Tr}\nolimits\big({-}u_{\alpha\gamma}v^{\prime\rm flat}_{\gamma}+v^{\rm flat}_{\alpha}u^{\prime}_{\alpha\gamma}\big)

\displaystyle\hphantom{{\rm d} \mathop{\rm Tr}\nolimits\big(u_{\alpha\beta}u'_{\beta\gamma}\big)=}{}+\mathop{\rm Tr}\nolimits\big({-}u_{\alpha\beta}v^{\prime\rm flat}_{\beta}+v^{\rm flat}_{\alpha}u^{\prime}_{\alpha\beta}\big),

\displaystyle{\rm d}\mathop{\rm Tr}\nolimits\big({-}u_{\alpha\beta}v^{\prime\rm flat}_{\beta}+v^{\rm flat}_{\alpha}u^{\prime}_{\alpha\beta}\big)=\mathop{\rm Tr}\nolimits\big(\nabla^{\rm flat}_{{\dagger}}\big({-}u_{\alpha\beta}v^{\prime\rm flat}_{\beta}+v^{\rm flat}_{\alpha}u^{\prime}_{\alpha\beta}\big)\big)

\displaystyle\hphantom{{\rm d}\mathop{\rm Tr}\nolimits\big( {-}u_{\alpha\beta} v'^{\rm flat}_{\beta} +v^{\rm flat}_{\alpha} u'_{\alpha\beta} \big)}{}=\mathop{\rm Tr}\nolimits\big({-}\nabla^{\rm flat}_{{\dagger}}(u_{\alpha\beta})\wedge v^{\prime\rm flat}_{\beta}-v^{\rm flat}_{\alpha}\wedge\nabla^{\rm flat}_{{\dagger}}(u^{\prime}_{\alpha\beta})\big)

\displaystyle\hphantom{{\rm d}\mathop{\rm Tr}\nolimits\big( {-}u_{\alpha\beta} v'^{\rm flat}_{\beta} +v^{\rm flat}_{\alpha} u'_{\alpha\beta} \big)}{}=\mathop{\rm Tr}\nolimits\big(\big(v^{\rm flat}_{\alpha}-v^{\rm flat}_{\beta}\big)\wedge v^{\prime\rm flat}_{\beta}\big)-\mathop{\rm Tr}\nolimits\big(v^{\rm flat}_{\alpha}\wedge\big(v^{\prime\rm flat}_{\beta}-v^{\prime\rm flat}_{\alpha}\big)\big)

\displaystyle\hphantom{{\rm d}\mathop{\rm Tr}\nolimits\big( {-}u_{\alpha\beta} v'^{\rm flat}_{\beta} +v^{\rm flat}_{\alpha} u'_{\alpha\beta} \big)}{}=-\mathop{\rm Tr}\nolimits\big(v^{\rm flat}_{\beta}\wedge v^{\prime\rm flat}_{\beta}\big)+\mathop{\rm Tr}\nolimits\big(v^{\rm flat}_{\alpha}\wedge v^{\prime\rm flat}_{\alpha}\big)

and

\displaystyle{\rm d}\mathop{\rm Tr}\nolimits\big(v_{\alpha}^{\rm flat}\wedge v^{\prime\rm flat}_{\alpha}\big)=\mathop{\rm Tr}\nolimits\big(\nabla^{\rm flat}_{{\dagger}}\big(v^{\rm flat}_{\alpha}\wedge v^{\prime\rm flat}_{\alpha}\big)\big)=0.

Therefore,

\big[\big\{\mathop{\rm Tr}\nolimits(u_{\alpha\beta}u^{\prime}_{\beta\gamma}),-\mathop{\rm Tr}\nolimits\big(u_{\alpha\beta}v^{\prime\rm flat}_{\beta}+v^{\rm flat}_{\alpha}u^{\prime}_{\alpha\beta}\big),\mathop{\rm Tr}\nolimits\big(v^{\rm flat}_{\alpha}\wedge v^{\prime\rm flat}_{\alpha}\big)\big\}\big]

defines an element of

\mathbb{H}^{2}\big(\Omega^{\bullet}_{{\mathcal{C}}_{y\times{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime}}}\big)\cong\mathbb{C}

and its image by the map (
11.8
)
coincides with

\omega\big(\tilde{v},\tilde{v^{\prime}}\big)=\big[\big\{\mathop{\rm Tr}\nolimits(u_{\alpha\beta}u^{\prime}_{\beta\gamma}),-\mathop{\rm Tr}\nolimits(u_{\alpha\beta}v^{\prime}_{\beta}+v_{\alpha}u^{\prime}_{\alpha\beta})\big\}\big].

Thus we have proved the corollary.
∎
